  5. **Mandatory present-directory/scope-root verification → scope:** every mandatory present-directory root (`git-dir-control`, `common-dir-control`, `common-refs`, `worktree-control`) is verified through its exact `recursive_scope_proofs` literal key and its `stability_entries` root/present-directory record, with `entry_count >= 1` for every present-root scope. **`common-replace-refs` is a mandatory tagged scope, not a mandatory present-directory root** (V9 disposition item 4): the compiler requires the literal key `common-replace-refs` to be present in `recursive_scope_proofs` in every admitted descriptor and verifies that its shape is exactly one of the two admitted shapes of the frozen state machine; it does not require `(common_dir,"refs/replace")` to be a present-directory record, and in the absent-root variant it requires the scope's `witness_ref` to resolve (serialized edge 4) while in the present-empty variant it requires the single root stability record and `entry_count=1`. Scope-root verification is a compiler obligation over the required scope record, not another serialized reference edge; edges 1–4 are the complete serialized edge set.

**Exact consumer sets (frozen; V9 disposition item 4).** Every stored witness in `component_probe_witnesses` has exactly one consumer. For the observed linked profile (absent-root `common-replace-refs` variant): `W(git_dir,"config.worktree")` ← absent `control_files` record `git_dir/config.worktree`; `W(common_dir,"shallow")` ← absent `control_files` record `common_dir/shallow`; `W(common_dir,"packed-refs")` ← absent `control_files` record `common_dir/packed-refs`; `W(git_dir,"index.lock")` ← Class P rule `index-lock`; `W(git_dir,"HEAD.lock")` ← Class P rule `head-lock`; `W(common_dir,"config.lock")` ← Class P rule `config-lock`; `W(common_dir,"packed-refs.lock")` ← Class P rule `packed-refs-lock`; `W(common_dir,"shallow.lock")` ← Class P rule `shallow-lock`; `W(common_dir,"objects/info/alternates")` ← Class P rule `alternates`; `W(common_dir,"info/grafts")` ← Class P rule `grafts`; `W(common_dir,"info/attributes")` ← Class P rule `common-info-attributes`; `W(git_dir,"info/attributes")` ← Class P rule `git-info-attributes`; `W(common_dir,"refs/replace")` ← the absent-root scope record `common-replace-refs` (serialized edge 4). **Present-empty `common-replace-refs` variant:** `refs/replace` is present, so `W(common_dir,"refs/replace")` is not stored and has no consumer; the frozen absence set for that variant excludes `refs/replace`, and the witness set is the twelve witnesses of the three absent mandatory controls plus the nine Class P checked paths. Every stored scope in `recursive_scope_proofs` has its exact nonempty consumer set: `git-dir-control` ← {Scope G root verification}; `common-dir-control` ← {Scope C root verification}; `common-refs` ← {Scope R root verification, Class R rule `refs-locks`}; `common-replace-refs` ← {Scope RP root verification, Class R rule `replace-refs`} (both variants; in the absent-root variant the scope record additionally carries the outgoing serialized edge 4 to `W(common_dir,"refs/replace")`); `worktree-control` ← {Scope W root verification, Class R rule `worktree-attributes`}.

**`stability_sha256` canonical preimage (frozen, recomputable).** `stability_sha256 = SHA-256(stability_entries stream)`, where the stream is the byte concatenation of each record's compact lexical-key JSON plus one LF in array order. The compiler recomputes this digest from `stability_entries` and compares; any drift is `authority_repository_control_unsupported`.

**Each `absence_proofs[i]` canonical preimage (frozen, recomputable).** For rule `R`, the observation stream is the byte concatenation, in order, of the derived header record `{"rule_id":R,"passed":true,"observation_count":c}` and then every record of `proof_observations[R]`, each as compact lexical-key JSON plus one LF, where `c` is the length of `proof_observations[R]`; `observation_sha256 = SHA-256(stream)`. The compiler derives the header from `(rule_id, passed, observation_count)` and recomputes the digest from `proof_observations[R]`; it then validates every observation's exact shape and cross-references (each `{"witness_ref":H}` must resolve to a `component_probe_witnesses` key whose `(namespace, requested_path)` matches the rule's frozen checked path; each `{"scope_ref":K}` must resolve to a `recursive_scope_proofs` literal key whose logical root matches the rule's frozen scope root). The declared `source_kind` vocabulary is exactly `{worktree, git_dir, common_dir, index, command, config}`; the declared namespace vocabulary for control records and stability entries is exactly `{worktree, git_dir, common_dir}` (there is no undefined `index` namespace; index-derived observations use `source_kind:"index"`).

**Per-rule observation shapes (exact).**

*Class P — fixed-path absence (rules `index-lock`, `head-lock`, `config-lock`, `packed-refs-lock`, `shallow-lock`, `alternates`, `grafts`, `common-info-attributes`, `git-info-attributes`).* Exactly one observation record per checked path, and that record is exactly `{"witness_ref":H}` (the 64-hex witness key of the component-probe witness for the checked path). Records sorted by the byte-lexical witness key of the referenced witness. The compiler recomputes each witness's key and parent digest `Hp`, verifies the present parent entry (root record for `Q=""`, present-directory control record otherwise), exact path reconstruction, parent-chain termination at the root logical key, completeness, uniqueness, and the frozen consumer set; it rejects any missing/extra/duplicate witness or edge. Frozen checked paths: `index-lock` → `(git_dir, "index.lock")`; `head-lock` → `(git_dir, "HEAD.lock")`; `config-lock` → `(common_dir, "config.lock")` plus, when `extensions.worktreeConfig=true`, `(git_dir, "config.worktree.lock")`; `packed-refs-lock` → `(common_dir, "packed-refs.lock")`; `shallow-lock` → `(common_dir, "shallow.lock")`; `alternates` → `(common_dir, "objects/info/alternates")`; `grafts` → `(common_dir, "info/grafts")`; `common-info-attributes` → `(common_dir, "info/attributes")`; `git-info-attributes` → `(git_dir, "info/attributes")`.

*Class R — recursive offender enumeration (rules `refs-locks`, `replace-refs`, `worktree-attributes`).* Observation stream = one reference record `{"scope_ref":K}` followed by offender records, where `K` is the exact literal scope key of the rule's frozen scope root: `refs-locks` → `common-refs`; `replace-refs` → `common-replace-refs`; `worktree-attributes` → `worktree-control`. For the present-root shape, `scope_stability_sha256` is SHA-256 over the exact byte concatenation of (a) every stability entry record whose logical `(namespace,path)` is the scope root or lies under the scope subtree, in `stability_entries` order, and (b) every offender record in sorted order, each compact lexical JSON plus one LF. For the absent-root shape, (a) is empty, `entry_count=0`, `scope_stability_sha256` is SHA-256 of the empty concatenation, and the scope record carries `witness_ref` (never an embedded witness). Offenders (sorted byte-lexically by `path`, duplicate-free): `refs-locks` → `{"kind":"ref-lock","source_kind":"common_dir","path":P}` per discovered `refs/**/*.lock`; `replace-refs` → `{"kind":"replace-ref","source_kind":"common_dir","path":P}` per discovered entry under `refs/replace/`; `worktree-attributes` → `{"kind":"worktree-attributes","source_kind":"worktree","path":P}` per discovered `.gitattributes`. Every admitted descriptor has an empty offender list (`passed:true`); a present `refs/replace` with any entry fails the PR 7 preflight closed before any descriptor is produced.

*Class I — command/index proofs (rules `gitlinks`, `assume-unchanged`, `skip-worktree`).* Observation stream = one command-projection record followed by offender records. Command-projection record: `{"command_id":CID,"source_kind":"index","record_count":N,"stdout_byte_count":B,"stdout_sha256":H,"offender_count":c}` (6 keys), where `CID` is the rule's frozen command (`gitlinks` → `ls-tree-head`; `assume-unchanged` → `ls-files-v`; `skip-worktree` → `ls-files-t`), `N`/`B`/`H` are that command's `record_count`/`stdout_byte_count`/`stdout_sha256` from `command_results` (the compiler cross-checks them), and `c` is the offender count. Offenders (sorted byte-lexically by `path`, duplicate-free): `gitlinks` → `{"kind":"gitlink","source_kind":"index","path":P}` per 160000-mode tree entry; `assume-unchanged` → `{"kind":"assume-unchanged","source_kind":"index","path":P}` per lowercase `-v` tag; `skip-worktree` → `{"kind":"skip-worktree","source_kind":"index","path":P}` per `S` `-t` tag.

*Class C — config proofs (rules `config-includes`, `unknown-config`).* Observation stream = one config-scope record followed by offender records. Config-scope record: `{"config_sha256":Hc,"config_worktree_sha256":Hcw,"normalized_entry_digest":Hn}` (3 keys), where `Hc` is the digest of the `common_dir/config` control record's bytes, `Hcw` is the digest of the `git_dir/config.worktree` control record's bytes or `null` when absent (the compiler cross-checks both against `control_files`), and `Hn` is the `normalized_entry_digest` of §3.2. `config-includes` offenders: `{"kind":"config-include","namespace":NS,"file":F,"directive":D,"key":K}` (5 keys) per include/includeIf directive (`NS ∈ {common_dir, git_dir}`, `F ∈ {config, config.worktree}`, `D ∈ {include, includeIf}`, `K` = full normalized key), sorted by `(namespace, file, key)`. `unknown-config` offenders: `{"kind":"unknown-config-key","namespace":NS,"key":K}` (3 keys) per non-admitted normalized key, **sorted by `(namespace, key)` — not by path**.


**V28 config-proof preimage and Class-C semantics.** The Class-C `normalized_entry_digest` remains the digest of the unchanged three-field record stream; the value digest inside each record is now normatively the semantic-scalar digest above. PR 7 emits the config-scope record only after strict lexical normalization and recomputes `Hc`, `Hcw`, and `Hn`; Stage 19 verifies `Hc`/`Hcw` against `control_files`, recomputes `Hn` from `config_entries`, compares every known semantic key with the frozen zero/false/true digest set, and enforces the absent/false/true `config.worktree` state machine. An offender from include syntax, duplicate normalized key, unknown grammar/key, invalid scalar token, invalid string escape, or state-coupling drift prevents an admitted descriptor; a type-correct descriptor drift that reaches the compiler maps to `authority_repository_control_unsupported` at the exact config entry, control-file, proof, or lock-observation pointer.


**Descriptor-internal ownership partition — stage 1–10 versus stage 19 (V9 disposition item 2, frozen; V14 item 1 extends the nested-type subrule).** For every `repository_control` defect class, exactly one of stages 1–10 or stage 19 owns the defect; no descriptor defect satisfies two stage predicates and no wrong container/scalar type falls through every stage. The partition is:

| Descriptor defect class | Owner | Exact failure pointer (stage 19 unless noted) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| JSON lexical/parse failure in the snapshot document, including bytes inside `repository_control` (invalid UTF-8, BOM, CR, missing final LF, syntax error, float/NaN/Infinity, surrogate, recursion failure, noncanonical escape) | stage 1 | `/<doc-token>` of the snapshot |
| duplicate object keys anywhere, including inside `repository_control` | stage 3 | `/<doc-token>/<escaped-object-path>/<escaped-key>` per duplicate occurrence |
| whole-snapshot-document byte re-encoding equality (canonical compact lexical-key JSON of the complete snapshot, including descriptor JSON key lexicality, spacing, and escaping) | stage 6 rule (i) | `/<doc-token>` per input |
| container shape of a descriptor field (object/array/string/number/boolean/null as frozen for that field) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field>` |
| missing/extra descriptor fields | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field>` |
| scalar types of descriptor fields (u64 vs string vs boolean vs null as frozen) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field-path>` |
| descriptor enum grammar (`worktree_kind`, `namespace`, `presence`, `type`, `kind`, `scope_state`, `control_name_key_rule`) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field>` (e.g. `worktree_kind`) |
| descriptor const-value grammar (`schema_version` ≠ const `turnvector.benchmark.repository-control.v1`; `strict_parser_version` ≠ const `canonical-strict-parser-v1`; any other value) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field>` (e.g. `strict_parser_version`) |
| descriptor digest grammar (64-hex) and u64 scalar domain (out-of-u64-domain value, negative, or noncanonical decimal) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field-path>` |
| descriptor path grammar — identity/root `absolute_path` uses the strict absolute POSIX string grammar; all other descriptor paths use their frozen relative POSIX grammar and field-specific root-empty allowance; reject traversal, empty interior components, backslash, NUL/noncanonical Unicode, invalid trailing slash, and byte-cap drift | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field-path>` |
| descriptor symlink/type claims (`worktree_git_entry.kind`, stability record `type`, descriptor `file_type`-like claims, witness fail-closed conditions) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/worktree_git_entry/kind` or `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/stability_entries/<i>` |
| descriptor-internal record ordering (control_files `(namespace,path)`, ignore_files `path`, stability_entries canonical order and fixed root order, proof_observations order, component_probe_witnesses byte-lexical key order, config_entries `(namespace,key)`, command_results fixed order, absence_proofs fixed order) | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<array>/<i>` per out-of-order record |
| descriptor cross-field/recomputation consistency (stability_sha256, observation_sha256, witness keys, parent digests, root-as-parent lookup, projection equality, reference graph and consumer sets, mandatory file/directory verification, `worktree_kind`-presence consistency, head/clean equality, namespace ownership, scan_counts, command projections, normalized_entry_digest, scope-proof shapes incl. the two admitted `common-replace-refs` shapes) | stage 19 | the exact subrule pointers of the stage-19 row in §10 |
| **nested-type cross-field agreement for every `ControlFileIdentity`/`StabilityIdentity` instance in the snapshot, including `SourceFileRecord.no_follow_identity` (type-correct, in-domain values; `size == byte_count`; exact 8-key shape; regular-file semantic)** | stage 19 | `/authority_snapshot/source_files/<i>/no_follow_identity/size` (snapshot-side instance) or `/authority_snapshot/repository_control/<field-path>` (descriptor-side instances) |

Stages 1–10 apply to `repository_control` **only** through stages 1, 3, and 6 rule (i) above; no other stage-1–10 predicate reads descriptor fields, and stage 19 never re-owns a defect already owned by stages 1, 3, or 6 rule (i) (stage 19 excludes duplicate keys and whole-document byte re-encoding; those fire earlier). For the snapshot-side `SourceFileRecord.no_follow_identity` instance, the three-stage split is total: stage 2 owns missing/unknown/mistyped nested fields, stage 4 owns out-of-u64-domain typed values, and stage 19 owns the type-correct in-domain cross-field agreement (`size == byte_count`) — a type-correct counterexample can no longer fall through (V14 item 1). The non-descriptor predicates are re-audited for the same totality and exclusivity property in §10.

The compiler validates closed shape, required proof/command sets, checked caps, ordering, command-to-snapshot projections, equality of descriptor head/clean with the top level, `ControlFileIdentity` and `StabilityIdentity` shapes (`size==byte_count`, u64 fields), `worktree_kind` consistency with the mandatory record presence rules (linked ⇒ `commondir`/`gitdir` present; main ⇒ both absent), `stability_entries` completeness/sort/root-order/parent-chain (present entries only; parent chains terminate at root logical keys), `control_files`/`ignore_files` projection equality, the exact reference graph (witness/scope consumer sets), every component-probe witness (recompute key and parent digest, verify reconstruction, completeness, uniqueness, root-as-parent rule), every scope proof (present/absent shape, literal scope key, entry count including the root record, digest preimage, and the frozen `common-replace-refs` state machine), and **recomputes `stability_sha256` and every `observation_sha256` from descriptor fields**; any recomputation drift is `authority_repository_control_unsupported`. PR 7 must recompute the complete descriptor — including every digest from the exact preimages above — from the physical repository immediately before publication and fail-stop on any drift. An inconsistency after qualified START maps to `authority_repository_control_unsupported`; an unsupported facility before START remains the custody refusal. CoveragePlan binds the canonical descriptor digest and parser version (§5.2). **V28 Stage-19 refinements:** identity/root absolute paths are checked as strings under the frozen absolute grammar and matched member-for-member; known config values are checked against the frozen semantic digest constants and the complete `extensions.worktreeConfig` state coupling; opaque config strings remain digest bindings only; and `command_results[].argv` receives structural/canonical string-array validation and provenance binding only, with no execution authorization, reconstruction, inference, or command-line equality test.

**Admitted/disallowed enumeration (complete).** Admitted control entries: `.git` indirection (gitfile or directory); `index`; `HEAD`; `config`; `config.worktree` (iff `extensions.worktreeConfig=true`); `info/exclude`; `shallow` (presence or absence); `packed-refs` (presence or absence); `commondir` and `gitdir` (present iff linked, explicit absence iff main); the bounded `refs/` subtree (present files and directories under `common_dir/refs/`); bounded worktree `.gitignore` files. `refs/replace` is admitted only as an empty directory (present-empty scope) or an absence (absent-root scope); any entry under it is an offender and fails preflight closed. Nothing under `common_dir/worktrees/` is admitted, enumerated, or bound. Admitted metadata-only config keys (closed grammar): `core.repositoryformatversion=0`, `core.bare=false`, bound booleans `core.filemode`, `core.ignorecase`, `core.precomposeunicode`, `core.logallrefupdates`, `extensions.worktreeConfig` (absent/false, or true with a separately bound and strictly parsed `config.worktree`), and the Git-inert keys `user.name`, `user.email`, `user.signingkey`, `gpg.format`, `gpg.program`, `commit.gpgsign`, `tag.gpgsign`, `core.sshCommand`, `remote.<name>.url/pushurl/fetch`, `branch.<name>.remote/merge`, plus the one application-only key `codex.localEnvironmentConfigPath` (ignored by Git, exact normalized Unicode string digest-bound). Disallowed (rejected or proven absent): `include`/`includeIf` directives, continuation syntax, duplicate normalized keys, invalid value domains, `alias.*`, `filter.*`, `diff.*.(command|textconv)`, `merge.*.driver`, `credential.*`, `core.attributesFile`, `core.excludesFile`, `core.fsmonitor`, `core.hooksPath`, `submodule.*`, `protocol.*`, `url.*`, `http.*`, `ssh.*`, `pager.*`, `interactive.*`, `sequence.*`, and every other unknown key; every attributes source (`common-dir info/attributes`, `git-dir info/attributes`, any worktree `.gitattributes`); grafts (`info/grafts`); alternates (`objects/info/alternates`); replacement refs under `refs/replace/`; ref locks under `refs/**/*.lock`; gitlinks, assume-unchanged, and skip-worktree index state (each proven by its Class I command proof with an empty offender list). **V28 normalization rule:** these semantic keys are admitted only after the strict one-line PR 7 lexical normalization above; booleans become canonical JSON booleans, `core.repositoryformatversion` becomes semantic unsigned zero, and each opaque value becomes an exact Unicode JSON string before its digest is computed. Opaque digests are never inverted by the compiler. Absence/false/true `extensions.worktreeConfig` states drive the already-frozen `config.worktree` control-file, witness, Class-C, and config-lock proof shapes.

---

## 4. Resolution 3 — process-bound single-use CompilePermit (V14 items 6–7; V15 item N7)

`CompilePermit` is created only by the CompileCustody wrapper (PR 7) after the START event object is durably committed, and is consumed exactly once by exactly one compiler invocation in the same process. PR 6 defines the value object, its consumption semantics, the issuance-kind mechanism, and the exception taxonomy; PR 7 wires creation and VIOLATION publication.

**Exact token contract (V14 item 6, frozen; replaces the contradictory V13 story).** Exactly two module-level token objects exist in `compile_permit.py`, both created at import time, and they are the complete token set:

- `_PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN` — the production entry identity. It is simultaneously (a) the issuer-authorization token required by the production factory and (b) the compiler-entrypoint token captured by a production permit. There is no separate "issuer token created by CompileCustody" and no separate "authorization token" — the single module-level object plays both roles, and every comparison is an identity comparison (`is`) against it.
- `_TEST_ISSUER_TOKEN` — the test entry identity, used by the test factory and captured by test permits.

No other token object exists; no permit can carry a token other than one of these two. The token each permit stores is exactly the token its factory received.

`CompilePermit` is an unexported mutable capability object, not a serializable authority schema. It holds one frozen `PermitPayload`, the issuing PID (`os.getpid()` at issuance), the exact entry-token reference (`_PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN` or `_TEST_ISSUER_TOKEN`), and a lock-protected consumed bit. `consume()` atomically changes false to true and returns the payload once. Copy, deepcopy, pickle, public construction, and subclassing fail. Python privacy is an in-process ownership boundary under the accepted same-user trust model, not a security claim.

**Token disclosure (V15 item N7, frozen).** The two tokens are module-level attributes of `compile_permit.py`; any same-user module can import them directly (e.g. `from turnvector_benchmark.authority.compile_permit import _PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN`) and could call `_issue_compile_permit(payload, token)` with the real token. The disclosure helpers `_production_entry_token()`/`_test_issuer_token()` and their caller-frame checks are therefore **misuse-detection/convention under the accepted same-user trust model, not unbypassable authority**: they detect accidental cross-module misuse and raise `CompilerInternalError` for any caller that is not the frozen caller, but they are not a security boundary and no unbypassability is claimed. The design does **not** state that the production token "is disclosed only through" the helper. Production enforcement is the stored-token identity check at `compile()` (state S4: `permit._entry_token is _PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN`), the single-consumption lock (S1/S2), and the issuer-PID check (S3), all of which remain exact and implementable. The two-token state machine (S1–S10 below) is unchanged and complete.

`PermitPayload` has exactly 24 fields: `issuance_kind` (`"production"` or `"test"`), `custody_domain_id`, `custody_domain_sha256`, `custody_lineage_id`, `attempt`, `t_max`, `start_event_sha256`, `chronology_prefix_sha256`, `chronology_prefix_byte_count`, `compiler_build_sha256`, `execution_closure_sha256`, `compile_custody_policy_sha256`, `authority_snapshot_sha256`, `authority_snapshot_byte_count`, `source_reconciliation_sha256`, `source_reconciliation_byte_count`, `expectation_sha256`, `expectation_byte_count`, `catalog_sha256`, `catalog_byte_count`, `traceability_sha256`, `traceability_byte_count`, `compile_limits_sha256`, and `input_set_sha256`. Every digest is lowercase 64-hex, every byte count is a checked u64, `attempt` is `1..t_max`, and `t_max=8`. The five byte counts are the exact expected lengths of the five serialized input buffers (§9, §10) so that metadata-only length identity can be established before hashing. **For the F46/B2″ family, the permit payload's `expectation_sha256` field is `D_exp_syn` (the synthetic expectation variant's digest) and `input_set_sha256` is recomputed over the synthetic inputs (V25 item 1, §15); every other payload field uses the fixture's frozen values.**

**PermitPayload construction boundary (V14 item 7, frozen).** `PermitPayload` is a frozen value object whose `__post_init__` validates every field at construction: every byte-count field is a **nonnegative checked u64** (a value outside the u64 domain — negative, ≥ 2^64, or a non-integer — raises `CompilerPreconditionViolation("input_identity_mismatch")`, the accepted VIOLATION reason for input identity); every digest is lowercase 64-hex; `attempt` is `1..t_max`; `t_max == 8`. The out-of-u64-domain byte-count condition is therefore **representable exactly at the permit-creation boundary**: a PermitPayload carrying such a value cannot be constructed, and the failure is a precondition raised before any compiler call — never a ContractFailure variant. At the **consumption boundary** (§4 step 5), the metadata-only identity check compares the O(1) `nbytes` of each admitted buffer against the payload byte counts; any disagreement is `CompilerPreconditionViolation("input_identity_mismatch")`. The frozen failure algebra (§10) explicitly excludes permit payload fields: stage 4 owns u64-domain failures only in the five serialized authority documents, the diagnostic model's synthetic five-document root never addresses permit fields, and a permit-condition failure is never serialized as a ContractFailure.

**Production issuance (frozen, V28 wording corrected).** `compile_permit._issue_compile_permit(payload, token)` is intended for the future `turnvector_benchmark.compile_custody` caller and guarded by convention/misuse detection, but it is an importable in-process function: a same-user module can import `_PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN` and call it with the real token. It requires `token is _PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN`; any other token raises `CompilerInternalError` as an issuance-time internal invariant failure. `compile_permit._production_entry_token()` raises `CompilerInternalError` unless the immediate caller's module `__name__` is exactly `turnvector_benchmark.compile_custody` (verified from the caller frame). Neither guard is a security boundary or a claim that other same-user modules cannot call the function. The factory validates the payload, sets internal `issuance_kind="production"` bookkeeping, stores the token reference, records `os.getpid()`, and returns the permit; `issuance_kind` is not copied into either compiler output.

**Test issuance (frozen, V28 wording corrected).** `compile_permit._issue_test_compile_permit(payload, token)` requires `token is _TEST_ISSUER_TOKEN`; any other token raises `CompilerInternalError`. The token helper raises `CompilerInternalError` unless the immediate caller's module `__name__` starts with `tests.`. The factory sets internal `issuance_kind="test"` fixture provenance and forces the payload's custody lineage/domain fields to the synthetic fixture's values. The tests-only helper module `tests/fixtures/compiler/test_permit_issuer.py` wraps this as `issue_test_compile_permit(payload_fields)`. A test permit is never accepted by production `compile()` and its outputs are never custody-consumable: PR 7's wrapper calls only `compile()` (import-boundary test), `compile()` rejects any permit whose stored token is not `_PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN` with `CompilerPreconditionViolation("compiler_identity_mismatch")`, and the test permit's synthetic lineage/domain can never match the accepted catalog's bound domain.

**Consumption semantics at compiler entry (frozen state machine).** The complete state table is:

| State | Check | Outcome |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | first `consume()` | succeeds atomically (false→true), returns payload once |
| S2 | second `consume()` | `CompilerPreconditionViolation("permit_reuse")`; PR 7 publishes the accepted VIOLATION and rejects |
| S3 | issuer-PID check: `permit._issuing_pid != os.getpid()` | `CompilerInternalError` (fail-stop, never serialized; a cross-process permit is an internal invariant violation and implicates no custody context of the current process) |
| S4 | entry-token check: `permit._entry_token is not _PRODUCTION_ENTRY_TOKEN` at `compile()` | `CompilerPreconditionViolation("compiler_identity_mismatch")` for PR 7's accepted VIOLATION path (a test permit carries `_TEST_ISSUER_TOKEN` and fails here; a forged permit fails here) |
| S5 | mirror entry-token check at `_compile_test`: `permit._entry_token is not _TEST_ISSUER_TOKEN` | `CompilerInternalError` (a production permit reaching the test entry is an internal invariant violation) |
| S6 | `CompileLimits.is_frozen()` and its canonical digest equal `payload.compile_limits_sha256` | a changed limit vector is `input_identity_mismatch`; production compilation has no raised or reduced limit seam |
| S7 | metadata-only identity and cap checks (before any hashing, decoding, copying, or traversal): for each of the five buffers in fixed order (snapshot, source reconciliation, expectation, catalog, ledger), compare the O(1) `nbytes` with the permit's expected byte count — any disagreement is `input_identity_mismatch`; then check `nbytes <= largest_single_serialized_parser_input_max` and checked-total `B <= serialized_input_bytes_total_max` — any breach selects `resource_input_cap_exceeded` (stage 45) at checkpoint K0 with no byte hashed, decoded, copied, or traversed, and with the K0 observed-counter values of §6 (`serialized_input_byte_count = B`; every other counter 0) | see §10 stage 45 and §6 |
| S8 | after step S7 passes, hash each admitted buffer (`nbytes` bytes) and compare against the permit digests; any disagreement is `input_identity_mismatch`; recompute `input_set_sha256`; disagreement is `input_identity_mismatch` | the wrapper publishes VIOLATION for any of these; no compiler ContractFailure is fabricated for an alternate invocation. **S8 hashes the five buffers one at a time in the fixed buffer order; each live SHA-256 state is retained as 256 logical bytes through `reserve(256)` under X and is released immediately after its buffer's hashing completes, before the next state is retained and before the W_B pre-reservation; after the fifth state is released `used = 0`. At every S8 retention `used = 0`, so `0 + 256 = 256 <= 33,554,432 = x_max` — the logical x_max cap (stage 46) is structurally unreachable at S8, proved by arithmetic, not asserted. The S8 `reserve(256)` is a stage-46 firing point assigned to checkpoint K1 with the exact S8 counters; a physical `MemoryError` at an S8 state allocation fires stage 48 at K1 through the allocation-fault seam with `logical_arena_byte_count = 0` (pre-attempt `used = 0`) and the S8 counters (V19 item 1, preserved; V20 re-verifies; V21 re-verifies; V22 re-verifies; V23 re-verifies; V24 re-verifies; V25 re-verifies).** |
| S9 | after capability/input identity is established, catalog domain/lineage/policy inconsistencies are ordinary typed catalog failures in the fixed validation algebra | §10 stages 20–23 |
| S10 | bind the payload identity verbatim; prefix fields identify only the virtual chronology through START; final history fields are structurally impossible in both output schemas | §5, §6 |

The `issuance_kind` payload member is internal bookkeeping and test-fixture provenance only. It is absent from CoveragePlan and ContractFailure and is never copied into an output and is not an output field. Enforcement is the stored token identity (S4/S5), which subsumes a separate kind re-check without introducing a second mechanism.

`input_set_sha256` is SHA-256 over one compact lexical-key JSON object plus LF with exactly these 15 fields (all five O(1) input byte counts are cryptographically bound; the five digests and the existing custody fields are retained): `attempt`, `authority_snapshot_byte_count`, `authority_snapshot_sha256`, `catalog_byte_count`, `catalog_sha256`, `compile_limits_sha256`, `custody_domain_id`, `custody_domain_sha256`, `custody_lineage_id`, `expectation_byte_count`, `expectation_sha256`, `source_reconciliation_byte_count`, `source_reconciliation_sha256`, `traceability_byte_count`, `traceability_sha256`. Identifiers use stable grammar, digests lowercase hex, byte counts and attempt checked u64. The permit length-equality check (step S7) remains before cap, hash, decode, copy, or traversal. **For the F46/B2″ family, `expectation_sha256` in this preimage is `D_exp_syn` (V25 item 1, §15).**

`compile_limits_sha256` hashes compact lexical JSON plus LF of `{"limits":{<all 68 exact field-name/value pairs>},"schema_version":"turnvector.benchmark.compile-limits.v1"}` (2 top-level keys). No test-only vector can share the frozen digest.

**Exception taxonomy (V28, sole internal exception).** `CompilerPreconditionViolation` has exactly `permit_reuse`, `compiler_identity_mismatch`, and `input_identity_mismatch`, matching accepted VIOLATION reasons. `CompilerInternalError` is the one and only named internal-invariant/fail-stop exception: it owns issuance-token mismatch, issuer-PID mismatch (S3), the S5 mirror check, private encoder/decoder round-trip failure, caller-frame/host invariant failure, and every other modeled internal invariant failure. There is no alias, subclass alternative, second internal exception, or implementation choice. `CompilerInternalError` is never serialized and is disjoint from `CompilerPreconditionViolation` and all 48 ContractFailure variants; neither exception can become ContractFailure bytes. A native `MemoryError` outside the enumerated modeled Stage-48 allocations — including failure-result materialization, diagnostic/output bytes, or excluded host bookkeeping — may propagate unchanged as an excluded host allocation failure. That native built-in propagation is not a second named internal-invariant exception and remains outside the 48-variant algebra.

## 5. Resolution 4 — exhaustive canonical CoveragePlan field schema and nesting (documentation list; physical object order is recursive lexical order)

**Schema family:** `turnvector.benchmark.coverage-plan.v1`. **Serialization:** compact recursively lexical-key JSON plus one LF (§13). The following top-level field list is schema/documentation, not physical emission order. Strict top-level fields (all required, no unknowns), exactly 23: `schema_version` (const `turnvector.benchmark.coverage-plan.v1`), `profile_id` (const `turnvector-implementation-v2`), `outcome` (const `coverage_plan`), `custody_domain_id` (identifier, permit), `custody_domain_sha256` (sha256, permit), `custody_lineage_id` (identifier, permit; = catalog `compile_custody_lineage_id`), `attempt` (u64 >= 1, permit), `t_max` (u64 = 8, permit), `start_event_sha256` (sha256, permit), `chronology_prefix_sha256` (sha256, permit; through-START prefix only), `chronology_prefix_byte_count` (u64, permit), `compiler_build_sha256` (sha256, permit), `execution_closure_sha256` (sha256, permit), `compile_custody_policy_sha256` (sha256, permit), `input_set_sha256` (sha256, permit), `inputs` (object, exact input digests; §5.1), `source` (object, exact source revision/status/file/range records; §5.2), `entity_sets` (object, 14 sha256 fields; §5.3), `relation_sets` (object, 13 sha256 fields; §5.4), `counts` (object, exact fields; §5.5), `limits` (object, the 68 frozen CompileLimits field names/values verbatim), `expected_keys` (object, §5.6), `limitations` (array of bounded strings, ordered; §5.7).

The plan contains **no final compile-history digest or count** and no plan self-digest. It includes the complete canonical sorted expected key names, but commits large entity/relation sets by digest and count rather than embedding their records. Arrays retain the orders below; integers are canonical nonnegative decimal u64.

### 5.1 `inputs`
Exactly 11 fields: `source_reconciliation_path`, `source_reconciliation_sha256`, `expectation_path`, `expectation_sha256`, `catalog_path`, `catalog_sha256`, `traceability_path`, `traceability_sha256`, `authority_snapshot_sha256`, `compile_limits_sha256`, and `traceability_bindings`. The four paths are the accepted repository-relative path constants: `authority/source-reconciliation-v1.json`, `expectations/turnvector-implementation-v2.json`, `authority/obligation-catalog-v1.jsonl`, `authority/traceability-v1.json`. `traceability_bindings` reproduces the complete strict `binds` object so every lane-suite, case-schema, judge, bundle, raw-source, context, output, gate, and negative-template digest is individually bound — the three negative-template digest objects are keyed by the exact template ids (the record `id` values of §2.3). **For the F46/B2″ family, `expectation_sha256` is `D_exp_syn` and `traceability_bindings.expectation_sha256` is `D_exp_syn` (V25 item 1, §15); the F46/B2″ fixtures fail in Phase B or Phase C and never produce plan bytes, but the identity fields are frozen consistently.**

### 5.2 `source`
Exactly 14 fields: `repository`, `revision`, `observed_head`, `revision_relation`, `clean_required`, `clean`, `repository_control_sha256`, `repository_control_version`, `source_files`, `source_files_sha256`, `section_records`, `section_records_sha256`, `historical_objects`, and `historical_objects_sha256`. The display arrays have exact record fields: `source_files` uses `{path,byte_count,sha256,file_type}` (4 keys); `section_records` uses `{path,start,end,sha256}` (4 keys); `historical_objects` uses `{revision,path,byte_count,sha256,git_object_type}` (5 keys). They reproduce the corresponding snapshot records in canonical order; only `SourceFileRecord.no_follow_identity` is omitted from the display projection. Their set digests are computed only from the complete canonical AuthoritySnapshot record streams, including physical identity fields, and are copied alongside the display projections; neither the compiler nor a later validator may recompute those digests from the lossy displayed arrays. A later validator receives or reopens the bound AuthoritySnapshot to recompute them. The exact serialized bytes of the displayed arrays are included in the CoveragePlan output-byte accounting before the p_max guard. **`strict_parser_version` is not a CoveragePlan field (V15-N4 as applied by V16): it is a RepositoryControlDescriptor input field committed to the plan only indirectly through `repository_control_sha256`; no plan field, table, or test in this design names it as a plan-output field.**

**Frozen commitment preimages (V14 item 5, controlling; V19 re-verified; V20 re-verifies; V21 re-verifies; V22 re-verifies; V23 re-verifies; V24 re-verifies; V25 re-verifies).** The four source-phase commitments use the exact framing of §2.4 — SHA-256 over the byte concatenation of one compact recursively lexical-key JSON object plus exactly one LF (0x0A) per record, in the frozen record order, with no enclosing array, no separator between records, no leading/trailing bytes, strict UTF-8 — with these exact projections, sort orders, empty-stream identities, and standalone/nested relationships:

- **`repository_control_sha256`** — the digest of the **standalone canonical descriptor document**: SHA-256 over the compact lexical-key JSON serialization of the complete `repository_control` object plus one LF (exactly the bytes the strict serializer emits for the parsed descriptor value alone). **Nested relationship:** the descriptor object is embedded in the snapshot document; the commitment is over the standalone value bytes, and the whole-snapshot byte re-encoding equality (stage 6 rule (i)) binds the embedded bytes. The descriptor is never empty (22 mandatory fields), so the empty-stream identity is unreachable for this commitment. Sort order: none (single object). **This is the indirect commitment through which the frozen `strict_parser_version` const value is bound into the plan (V15-N4 as applied by V16).**
- **`source_files_sha256`** — records are the **complete** `SourceFileRecord` canonical values `{"path":P,"byte_count":B,"sha256":H,"file_type":"regular","no_follow_identity":{device,inode,uid,gid,mode,size,mtime_ns,ctime_ns}}` (the complete record **including** `no_follow_identity`, whose `size` equals `byte_count` by stage 19); order = the snapshot `source_files` array order, which is canonical sorted by `path` (byte-lexical). Empty-stream identity: `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855` (SHA-256 of zero bytes) is the defined digest of an empty record set; it is unreachable in any successful plan because the catalog always cites source paths and stages 11/25 force set equality (a catalog with 46 required obligations cannot have an empty `source_files`).
- **`section_records_sha256`** — records are the complete `SectionRecord` canonical values `{"path":P,"start":S,"end":E,"sha256":H}`; order = the snapshot `section_records` array order, which is canonical sorted by `(path, start, end)`. Empty-stream identity: `e3b0c442…`; unreachable in a success (every obligation carries a nonempty section, so the set is nonempty).
- **`historical_objects_sha256`** — records are the complete `HistoricalObjectRecord` canonical values `{"revision":R,"path":P,"byte_count":B,"sha256":H,"git_object_type":"blob"}`; order = the snapshot `historical_objects` array order, which is canonical sorted by `(revision, path)`. Empty-stream identity: `e3b0c442…`; unreachable in a success (stage 12 set equality forces the seven mappings' 14 sides).

**Standalone/nested relationship (frozen):** these four digests are computed over the standalone complete-record streams of the AuthoritySnapshot, never over the plan's lossy display projections and never over the whole snapshot document (except `repository_control_sha256`, which is the standalone descriptor value). The plan embeds only the lossy display arrays plus the digests; a later validator recomputes the digests from the bound AuthoritySnapshot. The compiler computes the four digests in the fixed order `repository_control_sha256`, `source_files_sha256`, `section_records_sha256`, `historical_objects_sha256` in the **Phase A source-commitment subphase of the frozen pipeline (§10, §11 — V15-N3 as applied by V16, preserved)** — the end of Phase A after stage 28 passes and before Phase B begins — streaming each preimage through the shared `h_max` hash buffer; the preimage bytes are bounded by the already-parsed snapshot bytes (a sub-part of `B`), so they add no new term to the work bound and are **not** part of `U_digest` (§16). **The `h_max` logical reservation at the Phase A subphase is a stage-46 firing point at K1 (position (f) counters, candidate `used + 65,536`) and a stage-48 firing point at K1 through the allocation-fault seam (position (f) counters, `logical_arena_byte_count = parse-retained + W_B` pre-attempt `used` — V20 item 2 as scoped by V21 item 2 and extended by V22 item 2: the physical `bytearray(65,536)` is allocated exactly once at the subphase start; the Phase D use is a second logical `reserve(65,536)` at K4 reusing that same physical bytearray with no physical allocation, no ordinal, and no stage-48 firing point).**

**`repository_control_version`** (V14 item 4) is the const `turnvector.benchmark.repository-control.v1`, copied verbatim from the validated descriptor's `schema_version` field; the compiler asserts `plan.repository_control_version == descriptor.schema_version == const` when building the plan (§3.2). It is a frozen constant value, part of `P_nonkey` — the **only** version constant that is a direct CoveragePlan output field (V15-N4 as applied by V16).

### 5.3 `entity_sets`
Exactly 14 fields: `lane_sha256`, `behavior_case_sha256`, `case_plan_sha256`, `obligation_sha256`, `judge_sha256`, `evidence_bundle_sha256`, `evidence_source_sha256`, `lane_context_contract_sha256`, `post_gate_output_contract_sha256`, `gate_sha256`, `judge_negative_template_sha256`, `aggregate_negative_template_sha256`, `plumbing_negative_template_sha256`, and `enforcement_path_sha256` — one per §2.4 entity stream.

### 5.4 `relation_sets`
Exactly 13 fields: `behavior_obligation_sha256`, `obligation_judge_sha256`, `lane_bundle_sha256`, `obligation_gate_sha256`, `bundle_evidence_sha256`, `lane_context_sha256`, `lane_output_sha256`, `path_case_sha256`, `path_gate_sha256`, `path_evidence_sha256`, `path_judge_negative_sha256`, `gate_aggregate_negative_sha256`, and `gate_plumbing_negative_sha256` — one per §2.4 relation stream.

### 5.5 `counts` (all u64)
Exactly 41 fields: `lane_count`, `behavior_case_count`, `case_plan_count`, `obligation_count`, `judge_count`, `evidence_bundle_count`, `evidence_source_count`, `lane_context_contract_count`, `post_gate_output_contract_count`, `gate_count`, `judge_negative_template_count`, `aggregate_negative_template_count`, `plumbing_negative_template_count`, `obligation_gate_pair_count` (`M`), `enforcement_path_count` (`H`), `bundle_evidence_record_count` (52), `path_case_record_count` (`H`), `path_gate_record_count` (`H`), `path_evidence_record_count` (`R_HE`), `path_judge_negative_record_count` (`H`), `gate_aggregate_negative_record_count` (58), `gate_plumbing_negative_record_count` (58), `lane_context_record_count` (21), `lane_output_record_count` (22), `artifact_membership_total` (95), `relation_path_record_count` (`Q_path = 4H + R_HE + 211`), `relation_support_record_count` (`M + 104`), `relation_total_record_count` (`Q_total = 4H + R_HE + M + 315`), `endpoint_path_reference_count` (`R_path = 11H + 2R_HE + 422`), `endpoint_support_reference_count` (`2M + 208`), `endpoint_total_reference_count` (`R_total = 11H + 2R_HE + 2M + 630`), `entity_record_count` (`H + 852`), `relation_only_record_count` (`3H + R_HE + M + 315`), `serialized_input_byte_count` (`B`), `authority_file_count`, `authority_byte_count` (`A`), `section_count`, `section_byte_count` (`S`), `logical_arena_byte_count` (`X`), `key_count_exec` (`K_exec`), and `key_count_run` (`K_run`).

The two contract-type counts are exact constants: `lane_context_contract_count=2` for the types `manifest` and `environment`, and `post_gate_output_contract_count=2` for `report` and `checksums`. The relation membership counts are distinct: `lane_context_record_count=R_LC=21` and `lane_output_record_count=R_LP=22`. Contract-type counts never substitute for lane-local membership records. The plan uses the path metrics (`relation_path_record_count`, `endpoint_path_reference_count`) for the preserved parent subgraph and the total metrics (`relation_total_record_count`, `endpoint_total_reference_count`, `entity_record_count`, `relation_only_record_count`) for the full 27-stream accounting; the work bound (§16) uses the totals.

### 5.6 `expected_keys`
Exactly 6 fields: `names`, `count`, `sha256`, `compile_key_count`, `execution_key_count`, and `run_global_key_count`. `names` is the complete duplicate-free byte-lexically sorted array of all `K_run=K_exec+2t+6` canonical keys; `count=len(names)=key_count_run`; `sha256` hashes each ASCII name plus LF in array order. `U_key` (§16) is exactly the byte length of that hash preimage: `U_key = Σ_{k∈names} (len(k_bytes) + 1)`. The serialized `names` array byte count is exactly `U_key + 2·K_run + 1` (two quote bytes per string, `K_run−1` comma bytes, two bracket bytes; the LF bytes counted inside `U_key` are not part of the JSON) — see §16. `compile_key_count=2t+1` (`compile-history` plus attempt/output for 1..t), `execution_key_count=K_exec`, and `run_global_key_count=5`. The three disjoint classifications cover `names` exactly. Digests without the names are rejected because the accepted parent requires the plan to commit the canonical sorted complete expected key names.

### 5.7 `limitations` (ordered, each <=1024 UTF-8 bytes, exactly four entries)
The compiler emits exactly these, in this order: (1) "plan proves closure relative to the accepted obligation catalog and exact source snapshot only"; (2) "no production behavior, adapter availability, or semantic completeness of source prose is claimed"; (3) "plan binds the through-START chronology prefix only; the final history digest/count is first persisted by RunEnvironment"; (4) "structural fixture readiness does not imply claim readiness".
