## 10. Validation/error precedence — exact mutually exclusive partition and exact deterministic bytes

The 48-name tuple below is the immutable public ordinal. Capability/precondition violations (§4) occur before ContractFailure construction and are never among the 48. A **metadata-only safety preflight** at compiler entry (§4 step S7) first uses the O(1) caller-owned byte lengths and checked u64 without hashing, decoding, copying, or traversing; if proceeding would violate an input byte cap, it selects `resource_input_cap_exceeded` (ordinal 45) at checkpoint K0 before any byte traversal, with the K0 observed-counter values of §6 (`serialized_input_byte_count = B`; every other counter 0). Source and section caps (ordinals 43/44) are checked as the admitted snapshot/catalog metadata parses (checkpoint K1), with the prefix-through-offender counters of §6. Inside the admitted envelope, semantic stages 1–40 run in exact ordinal order, with **resource preemption**: any checked arithmetic/platform/allocation guard that makes the current stage unsafe preempts at its resource ordinal (41–48) because the earlier semantic result is unestablished. Only the failing stage completes its bounded canonical-order diagnostic sweep; no later stage runs.

**Frozen pipeline phases (V14 item 9, controlling; V15-N2 as applied by V16 freezes the derivation order; V15-N3 as applied by V16 adds the Phase A source-commitment subphase and deletes undefined phase labels; V17 item B2 redesigns the phases into validation-only then template-dependent derivation; V17-B1 as applied by V18 freezes the W_B pre-reservation; V19 item 1 freezes the S8 hash-state accounting; V20 items 1–2 freeze the K1 three-class partition, the W_B wording, the stage-48 ownership for the enumerated modeled allocations, and the Phase B no-modeled-allocation invariant; V21 items 1–5 freeze the modeled-inventory boundary, the output-builder preallocation, the Phase B slab-offset access, and the equal-values note; V22 items 1–4 freeze the real case-id basis and exact recurrence, the single physical `h_max` allocation with two logical reservations, the EnforcementPath charge wording, and the 425 derived case-id intern substep; V23 items 1–2 freeze the exact-intern-subtotal scoping and recurrence start and the global intern-table collision rule for the generated-case-id substep; V24 items 1–5 freeze the collision-independent F46/B2″ termination chain, the complete collision-free fixture construction, the corrected live-basis proof, the exact C4 collision fixture, and the scoped collision statements; V25 items 1–5 freeze the F46/B2″ synthetic expectation variant and its digest ownership, the exact prior-intern-set enumeration rule, the reconciliation count classes, the V24 process facts, and the re-verified claims).** The compiler executes exactly six phases — Phase A, Phase B, Phase C, Phase D, Phase E, Phase F — in this order; the observed-counter checkpoints of §6 (K0–K4) and the arena schedule of §11 are defined over them:

- **Phase A (parse and parse-time stages, including the source-commitment subphase):** after the entry-preflight identity checks (S7/S8) pass, the **S8 hashing pass** hashes the five admitted buffers one at a time in the fixed buffer order, each through one live SHA-256 state retained as 256 logical bytes via `reserve(256)` under X and released immediately after that buffer's hashing completes, before the next state is retained and before the W_B pre-reservation; after the fifth state is released, `used = 0` (V19 item 1). The **W_B pre-reservation** (2,712 bytes) is then the **first arena retention inside the six phases A–F after S8** (the S8 hash states are entry-preflight retentions outside the six phases A–F, each released before the next and before the W_B reservation — V19-N2, resolved), executed immediately before the stage-1 parse pass, with `used = 0` proved by the explicit S8 release (V19 item 1); its logical candidate `0 + 2,712 = 2,712` can never breach `x_max`, and a physical `MemoryError` at the W_B allocation fires stage 48 at K1 with the pre-parse counters and `logical_arena_byte_count = 0` (V20 item 2, scoped by V21 item 2). Stage 1 parses all five inputs in fixed order (the parse pass of §9); stages 2–6 sweep in fixed input order and document order; **then, after all five documents have completed structural and stage-6 admission and before stage 7, the frozen late-Phase-A substep (V22 item 4, V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5) visits the 425 derived case-id identifiers in the frozen traversal order (expectation lane order → matrix order → generator Cartesian-product lexicographic dimension/value order; `{lane}.{matrix}.{ordinal:04d}` with the exact generator ordinal, ordinals continuing across matrices within a lane) under the global intern-table collision rule: each visit performs an allocation-free lookup in the existing global intern table — if identical bytes already have an intern entry, the visit reuses that reference (charge `0` new arena bytes, no modeled physical allocation, no allocation ordinal, and no stage-46/48 firing point for that visit); if the bytes are absent, the visit runs checked arithmetic, logically reserves exactly `16 + len(c)`, and performs one modeled physical intern-entry allocation (only this miss consumes the next allocation ordinal and has K1 position-(f) stage-46/48 ownership under the existing rules); the hit/miss sequence is a pure function of the admitted bytes and the frozen prior intern order; the general contract is 425 visits and zero to 425 allocations, and on the frozen collision-free basis/fixtures of V24 item 2 — including the F46/B2″ synthetic expectation variant of V25 item 1, whose 425 case ids are identical to the live basis — and the live basis of V24 item 3 all 425 visits are misses with the exact 26,618 charge; no CasePlan record is materialized**; stages 7–28 evaluation; then the **source-commitment subphase** — the single physical `bytearray(65,536)` `h_max` allocation at subphase start (a K1 46/48 modeled firing point with position (f) counters; V22 item 2), the four source-phase commitment preimages streamed, the logical charge released after the subphase while the physical bytearray stays in a private reusable slot.
- **Phase B (semantic validation sweep — validation-only; V17 item B2, redesigned; V17-B1 as applied by V18 closes the transient-reservation hole; V20 item 2 adds the no-modeled-allocation invariant; V21 items 2 and 4 freeze the slab-offset access):** after stage 28 passes, the sweep establishes stages 29–40 in ordinal order **without materializing any derived record** (no P, R_CH, R_GH, R_HE, R_HNJ, R_GNG, or R_GNP record, and **no expanded CasePlan record**, is retained, hashed, or counted as a record — V17-N3, resolved; the 425 case-id identifier strings were visited in the Phase A substep of V22 item 4 under the global collision rule of V23 item 2 as extended by V24 items 2–5 — on the frozen collision-free basis all 425 visits are misses — and are never CasePlan records). Stages 29–36 evaluate over the parsed structures. The **stage-37 boundary scan** streams the path skeleton once in the frozen derivation order (lane order → pair order → case-id order; per path its P-record fields and its R_HE membership through its bundle's R_DE join), accumulates the sweep counter block (§11 — a fixed-offset region of the pre-reserved W_B slab, live through stages 29–38, released before stage 39, V19 item 6), and evaluates the stage-37 drift checks with the documented exclusions. **Stage 38** evaluates degree caps (parsed-property caps at sweep position 0; derived-record caps at the first offending path record in the skeleton enumeration). **Stage 39** evaluates the ten base-domain emptiness checks. **Stage 40** evaluates the negative-template subrules in the frozen order of row 40 with the stage-40 mapping tables (built one at a time after the counter block is released, before any stage-40 table is built — V19 item 6); **no `unique` template lookup executes in Phase B**. Checkpoint K2; sweep-time resource preemptions 41/42 fire at K2; **stage 46 is structurally unreachable in Phase B for every admitted input, proven by the W_B invariant (V17-B1 as applied by V18): every Phase-B-admitted input satisfies `parse-retained <= x_max − W_B`, and the sweep transients are fixed-offset regions of the pre-reserved W_B slab, so `used = parse-retained + W_B <= x_max` at every Phase B instant — a known stage-40 defect therefore always fires at checkpoint K2 and can never be shadowed by a later stage-46 allocation; stage 48 has no class in Phase B because the sweep performs no modeled physical allocation (V20 item 2, V21 items 2 and 4)**.
- **Phase C (template-dependent derivation):** **only after every stage 1–40 predicate passes**, the complete bounded derivation runs once in the **frozen order: R_GNG and R_GNP records first in gate canonical `(lane_id, gate_id)` order (each via the unique validated template record); then lane order → pair order → case-id order, with each path's P record generated together with its R_CH/R_GH/R_HNJ records (R_HNJ via the unique validated judge template record) and its R_HE records generated in evidence-source order — the path's bundle memberships in `(bundle_id, evidence_source_id)` order** — retaining each as derived per the arena schedule of §11; **the W_B pre-reservation is released immediately before the first Phase C retention (V17-B1 as applied by V18), so Phase C `used = parse-retained + derivation charges`**; resource preemptions 41/42/46/48 fire at checkpoint K3 with the frozen-order partial derived counters of §6 (golden (c) = `320 + M`); **a stage-48 failure at a Phase C modeled retention fires at K3 with the frozen-order partial counters strictly before the failed record, pre-attempt `used` = `parse-retained + derivation charges before the failed record`, and `output_byte_count_attempted = 0`; the failed record is never retained, hashed, or counted (V20 item 2, scoped by V21 item 2)**. **The F46/B2″ termination proof (V24 item 1, preserved by V25 item 1): the potential Phase C total is `>= 2,784 + 4,254·8,899 + 24·41,883 = 38,864,322 > 33,554,432 = x_max` — a lower bound that deliberately assumes zero parse-retained and zero generated-case-id intern charge and is therefore valid for every possible hit/miss sequence; the exact first-exceed is defined by the deterministic recurrence of §11 starting from the actual complete parse-retained charge and following the actual deterministic hit/miss sequence, never from any displayed subtotal or lower bound. On the exact F46 fixture of V25 item 1, every stage 1–40 predicate passes (see §10 F46 row and §15), so Phase C is reached and stage 46 fires at the first Phase C reservation whose candidate `used + n` exceeds `x_max`.**
- **Phase D (digest streams):** the 27 canonical digest streams (§2.4) are computed once each in table order, streamed through the shared `h_max` hash buffer, each with one live SHA-256 state (one at a time; the state is a `reserve(256)` firing point at K4); **a physical `MemoryError` at a Phase D digest-state modeled allocation fires stage 48 at K4 with the complete derived counters and pre-attempt `used` = `parse-retained + full derivation charges` (V20 item 2, scoped by V21 item 2). The Phase D `h_max` use (V22 item 2) is a **logical** `reserve(65,536)` at K4 against the current X charge, reusing the exact physical bytearray allocated once in Phase A: a stage-46 firing point at K4 with the complete counters and candidate `used + 65,536`; **no physical allocation, no allocation ordinal, and no stage-48 firing point occurs in Phase D**; the logical charge is released after Phase D (a logical release, never a physical free or deallocation).**
- **Phase E (keys):** the 14 fixed sorted key streams are k-way merged; the sorted names array is written by offset/view writes into the **output-builder preallocation** (the k-way merge keeps O(1) state over the fixed 14 streams and performs no modeled physical allocation beyond the single output-builder preallocation). **The output-builder preallocation is one exact-size byte array allocated after the exact stage-47 candidate-total check passes and immediately before the first Phase E name write (V21 item 3): it is the single final-output modeled allocation ordinal (the last ordinal, after the 27 Phase D digest states), uses checkpoint K4 with the complete counters, receives offset/view writes in Phases E and F, and never grows or reallocates; the label and timing are identical in every statement of this design (V21 item 3).**
- **Phase F (output):** the final output object is serialized in the frozen emission order (§13) into the same preallocated builder by offset/view writes; stage 47 fires at the p-limit guard **before** the output-builder preallocation (a stage-47 failure produces no builder allocation and no builder object — V21 item 3); **a physical `MemoryError` at the output-builder preallocation fires stage 48 at K4 with the complete counters, `output_byte_count_attempted = 0`, and pre-attempt `used` = `parse-retained + full derivation charges` (the output builder is the P dimension, not charged to X) (V20 item 2, scoped by V21 item 2)**; stage 48 fires only through the allocation-fault seam at an enumerated modeled pipeline allocation (V21 item 2).

**Private-parser contract.** The private parsers raise only syntax-stage (stage 1) parse failures and retain pair-preserving structured data plus structural parse facts (including every duplicate-key occurrence, every array order, every per-lane count, and every empty set). They **never reject** ordering, duplicate entity/relation objects, per-lane count drift, empty semantic domains, or any other condition assigned to stages 5/6/24/29–40; no private parser throws before the variant dispatcher except for stage-1 byte-level faults, and duplicate keys are reported by the dispatcher at stage 3 from the retained facts.

**u64-domain ownership (V9 disposition item 6, frozen; V11 item 1 extended; V14 item 7).** A declared scalar whose JSON type is not the frozen type (e.g. a string, boolean, object, or array where a u64 is required) is a **mistyped-field** failure: stage 2 for non-descriptor fields, stage 19 for repository_control fields. **This class includes every non-string JSON value of snapshot `source_files[].file_type` and `historical_objects[].git_object_type` (V11 item 1): the frozen type of both fields is string, so a number, boolean, object, array, or null value is a stage-2 mistyped-field failure at the exact field pointer; stages 9/10 require type-correct strings and stage 2 excludes those type-correct semantic-value failures.** A declared scalar of the frozen JSON integer type whose value is outside the admitted u64 domain (negative, or >= 2^64, or noncanonical decimal) is a **grammar/range** failure: stage 4 for non-descriptor fields, stage 19 for repository_control fields. This rule applies across every input family — ledger `cardinalities`, snapshot `SourceFileRecord.byte_count`/`SectionRecord.start`/`end`/`HistoricalObjectRecord.byte_count`, catalog `section_start`/`section_end`, and every descriptor u64 field (identities, byte counts, `entry_count`, `scan_counts`, `command_results` byte counts, `absence_proofs.observation_count`) — with the exact pointer of the owning stage. **Permit payload byte counts are excluded from the ContractFailure algebra entirely (V14 item 7):** they are validated at the permit-creation boundary (§4) as checked u64 and at the consumption boundary by the metadata-only byte-count identity check; both are precondition/VIOLATION conditions, never ContractFailure stages. **Stage 41 is distinct:** it owns only checked arithmetic **overflow** (`checked_add`/`checked_mul` operand-sum/product overflow) over individually admitted u64 operands — including declared per-lane array checked-sums and declared derived-cardinality consistency arithmetic — and stage 42 owns only checked platform-size conversion overflow; neither owns type or domain rejection.

**Mutual-exclusivity structure.** Each stage's predicate is scoped and total: for any admitted input, exactly one variant fires — the first predicate that is true in the fixed order; each predicate is defined over inputs that passed all earlier stages. The scopes below make the partition non-overlapping: stage 2 excludes repository_control fields (stage 19), duplicate keys (stage 3), value grammar (stage 4), and **every type-correct file-type value of `source_files[].file_type` / `historical_objects[].git_object_type` (stages 9/10) — while owning their non-string (mistyped) JSON values at the exact field pointer (V11 item 1)**; stage 4 excludes path fields (stages 7/8, including the empty string at stage 8), file-type values (stages 2/9/10: stage 2 owns the non-string JSON types, stages 9/10 own the type-correct string values), repository_control fields (stage 19), **permit payload fields (precondition domain, V14 item 7)**, and owns the frozen non-descriptor const subrules (V15 item N4); stage 5 owns only the explicit closed scalar-ID uniqueness domains (including `source_files[].path` and **not** `section_records[].path`) plus the **catalog header-id vs body-id collision rule (V14 item 1)**, and excludes duplicate complete ledger entity/relation records (stage 30); **within the three negative-template arrays, stage 5 is the sole owner of every repeated template `id` — under the same `owner_id` or a different one, including an exact duplicate `{owner_id,id}` record — while two records sharing an `owner_id` with distinct `id`s are distinct records never owned by stage 5, and stage 30 explicitly excludes all three negative-template arrays (V13 item 2)**; stage 6 is evaluated only over distinct structurally valid records, with duplicates owned by stages 3/5/17/30 noted/skipped — **including skipping the ordering inspection of any negative-template array that contains a repeated `id` (stage 5 owns it), so stage 6 inspects `(owner_id,id)` ordering only after stage-5 repeated-id defects are absent**; stage 24 is a post-full-admission semantic count check (V15-N6 as applied by V16); stages 29–40 are validated in the Phase B sweep before any derivation, so no stage-40 defect can be shadowed by a later derivation-phase preemption (V17 item B2); rows 46/48 are the arena/allocation resource ordinals with the frozen classes and the modeled-inventory scope (V20 items 1–2, V21 items 1–2, V22 items 2 and 4, V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5, V25 items 1–5). A cross-domain equality between an input identifier and a derived case id — the C4 collision of V24 item 4 — satisfies no stage predicate (stage 5's domains are input-only and within-domain; the derived case id is not a member of any stage-5 domain; stage 29/30/37/40 fire only on input-side reference/duplicate/count defects that the equality does not create), so the C4 fixture passes stages 1–45 and the collision is observable only through the case-id substep's hit/miss behavior. **The F46/B2″ fixtures' 1,024-byte gate ids are input-side identifiers within their own stage-5 domains (distinct within the per-lane gate domain) and are referenced consistently by the ledger, so they satisfy no stage predicate other than the ordinary ones that pass; stage 37's per-lane gate-set equality is satisfied by construction on the synthetic expectation variant (V25 item 1).**

**Required-invariant ownership ledger (V14 item 1, controlling; totality proof; V15 items B3/N4, V16 items B1/B2/N1/N2/N3, V17 items B1/B2/N1/N2/N3, V17 items B1/N1/N2/N3/N4 as applied by V18, V19 items 1–6, V20 items 1–5, V21 items 1–5, V22 items 1–4, V23 items 1–2, V24 items 1–5, and V25 items 1–5 update rows 46 and 48 and the case-id intern entries to miss entries).** Every accepted invariant of the governing documents and schemas has exactly one owner stage; an input that violates no earlier predicate proceeds. **Diagnostic byte format (frozen).** Each diagnostic string is `<variant>|<json-pointer>|<reason-code>` per §6. JSON Pointer reference tokens are RFC 6901 escaped: `~` → `~0` then `/` → `~1` in every token. Diagnostic order is the failing stage's canonical examination order: stages 1–6 in fixed input order (snapshot, source reconciliation, expectation, catalog, ledger) and then declaration order within an input; stages 7+ in fixed input order and then canonical sorted record order and schema field order within each input (for stage 9/10, the snapshot `source_files` array in `path` order before the `historical_objects` array in `(revision, path)` order — V11 item 2; for stage 5, each negative-template array is swept in fixed array order `judge_negative_tests`, `aggregate_negative_tests`, `plumbing_negative_tests` with diagnostics at the index of each repeated `id` occurrence beyond the first; for stage 12, the snapshot `historical_objects` array in canonical `(revision, path)` order (extra tuple-distinct and duplicate-tuple/duplicate-complete diagnostics) and then the reconciliation mappings in array order (missing-side diagnostics) — V15 item B3; for stages 13/14/15, disagreeing fields in fixed input order; for stage 19, subrules in the frozen subrule order of the row; for stage 40, the frozen subrule order of row 40 — count-drift diagnostics in fixed array order `judge_negative_tests`, `aggregate_negative_tests`, `plumbing_negative_tests`, then judge mapping-count diagnostics in frozen path/owner order, then gate mapping-count diagnostics in gate canonical order with per-gate aggregate-then-plumbing interleaving — V16-N1, frozen; **for stage 48, the diagnostic is emitted at the exact allocation ordinal in the frozen modeled physical-allocation order of §11, with the failing class's single frozen counter set; the counter pointer is `/resources/allocation` in every class; no diagnostic is emitted for a fail-stop host `MemoryError` at an excluded allocation (V21 item 2); no diagnostic is emitted for a case-id intern hit visit because it creates no firing point (V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5)**.

**Frozen base-domain list for stage 39 (check order, exactly these ten):** (1) required lanes (the 12 expectation lanes); (2) required obligations O_p; (3) each lane's explicit behavior-case set; (4) the expanded CasePlan set C_p (**count computed from the frozen matrix dimensions without retention — V17-N3, resolved**); (5) the judge set J_p; (6) the gate set G_p; (7) the evidence-bundle set D_p; (8) the evidence-source set E_p; (9) the lane-context contract-type set (2); (10) the post-gate-output contract-type set (2). **Removed from stage 39:** the three negative-template sets (owned by stage 40) and every obligation-gate/bundle/context/output membership relation set (owned by stages 31/34/35/37).

with side multiplicity 2, the third and later occurrences); **(d) duplicate complete record** — a duplicated tuple whose record is byte-identical in all five fields to the earlier record (same variant, same pointer as (c); the sub-rule is distinguished for determinism but emits the identical diagnostic). **A 15-record list duplicating one of the 14 sides deterministically fails** (V15-N5 as applied by V16) |

Precedence guarantee: within an admitted safety envelope, simultaneous observable semantic defects select the smallest ordinal whose predicate is true; multiple resource defects select the smallest applicable resource ordinal among those observable at the current checkpoint; a resource guard encountered while establishing an earlier semantic stage selects that resource failure because the earlier semantic result is unestablished, never guessed. **Entry-preflight resource rule: at the S8 hashing pass, `used = 0` at every state retention, so the logical stage-46 guard cannot fire (proved: `0 + 256 = 256 <= 33,554,432`); a physical S8 state allocation failure fires stage 48 at K1 with the S8 counters; the W_B pre-reservation then executes as the first arena retention inside the six phases A–F after S8 with `used = 0` proved by the explicit S8 release, its logical 46 provably unreachable (`0 + 2,712`), and its physical failure a stage-48 firing point at K1 with the pre-parse counters (V19 items 1–2, V19-N2, V20 items 1–2, V21 item 2).** **Phase B resource-preemption rule: while the sweep is establishing stages 29–40, only checked-arithmetic/platform-size preemptions (41/42) are reachable; they report checkpoint K2 with the parsed-only counters; stage 46 cannot fire during the sweep — proven by the W_B invariant (V17-B1 as applied by V18) — and stage 48 has no class during the sweep because the sweep performs no modeled physical allocation (V21 items 2 and 4) — so no arena or modeled-allocation preemption can interrupt the semantic establishment other than 41/42 — and the semantic result is never guessed.** **Fail-stop host boundary in precedence: a native `MemoryError` at an excluded allocation (failure-result/diagnostic materialization, host object/header bookkeeping) is not a stage — it propagates unchanged as a fail-stop host failure outside the 48-variant algebra and never recurses into stage 48 (V21 item 2); the precedence rule therefore ranges over the 48 variants plus the fail-stop boundary, and a case-id intern hit visit contributes no firing point to any precedence analysis (V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5).**

## 11. Resource charging/checking algorithm and checked-u64 behavior (V14 item 10; V15 items B4/N2; V16 items B1/N3; V17 items B1/B2/N2; V17 items B1/N2/N3/N4 as applied by V18; V19 items 1–6; V20 items 1–5; V21 items 1–5; V22 items 1–4; V23 items 1–2; V24 items 1–5; V25 items 1–5)

- **Checked u64.** Every accumulation, product, count, range length, and conversion uses `compile_limits.checked_add` / `checked_mul` / `checked_nonnegative_u64` / `platform_size`; an overflow of admitted operands maps to `resource_checked_arithmetic_overflow` (or `resource_platform_size_overflow` for size conversion). A declared field whose JSON type is not the frozen type is a mistyped-field failure (stage 2 for non-descriptor fields — including non-string `file_type`/`git_object_type` values — stage 19 for descriptor fields), and a type-correct integer outside the u64 domain is a range failure (stage 4 for non-descriptor fields, stage 19 for descriptor fields); neither ever reaches checked arithmetic (V9 disposition item 6, V11 item 1). Permit payload byte counts are validated at the permit-creation boundary and never reach checked arithmetic in the compiler pipeline (V14 item 7). The checked sum of the five buffer `nbytes` values at the entry preflight is part of the K0 checkpoint; on overflow it selects stage 41 with `serialized_input_byte_count=0` (V15 item B1). The inner Phase B scratch guard `scratch_used + n <= W_B` is a checked-u64 comparison over fixed small constants (≤ 2,712), so it cannot overflow. The S8 hash-state `reserve(256)` is a checked-u64 comparison over the fixed constant 256 at `used = 0`, so it cannot overflow and cannot breach the logical x_max guard (V19 item 1). The W_B reservation is a checked-u64 comparison `0 + 2,712 <= x_max` at `used = 0`, so it cannot overflow and cannot breach the logical x_max guard (V20 item 2, V21 item 2). **The `h_max` logical `reserve(65,536)` is a checked-u64 comparison `used + 65,536 <= x_max` at its K1 (Phase A) and K4 (Phase D) firing points; the Phase D reservation reuses the single Phase A physical bytearray and adds no physical allocation (V22 item 2). The case-id intern miss reservation is a checked-u64 comparison `used + (16 + len(c)) <= x_max` after checked arithmetic establishes `16 + len(c)`; a hit visit performs no reservation and no checked comparison beyond the allocation-free lookup (V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5).**
- **Accounting ledger.** One object owns the counters in §6. `A=sum source_file.byte_count<=67,108,864`, file count <=256, each file <=4,194,304; `S=sum_catalog_records(end-start)<=33,554,432` with overlap multiplicity and at most 1024 records; `B` is the checked sum of exactly the five serialized buffers (snapshot, source reconciliation, expectation, catalog, ledger), each <=4,194,304 and total <=16,777,216. Entity/path/degree/output caps use the exact frozen fields. Every guard executes before the operation it protects.

**Complete frozen logical-arena model (V14 item 10 as corrected by V15 items B4/N2, V16 item N3, V17 items B2/N2, V17 items B1/N2/N3/N4 as applied by V18, V19 items 1–6, V20 items 1–5, V21 items 1–5, V22 items 1–4, V23 items 1–2, V24 items 1–5, and V25 items 1–5, controlling).** The arena charge `X = logical_arena_byte_count` is a **logical accounting contract**, not an RSS bound. Its interpretation is frozen as the **peak concurrent (high-water) charge**: at every instant, `used` is the sum of the charges of all structures live at that instant under the frozen phase sequence; `X` is the maximum `used` reached over the call (and for a stage-46 failure, the candidate `used + n` that first exceeds `x_max`; for a stage-48 failure, the pre-attempt retained `used` at the failed modeled reservation — the logical guard passed with the candidate `used + n` that was never retained). The compiler retains **exactly** the following structures, with exactly these charges, in exactly these lifetimes; there is **no alignment padding** — charges are exact byte sums — and no opaque record, Python object-header, allocator, interpreter, stack, or imported-page estimate is claimed (host object/header costs are outside the modeled inventory under the fail-stop host boundary of V21 item 2):

| Retained structure | Unit charge | Lifetime (creation → release) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Phase B scratch pre-reservation `W_B` (V17-B1 as applied by V18, preserved; V19 item 1 re-verifies; V20 item 2 assigns its stage-48 ownership; V21 items 2 and 4 freeze the slab implementation):** the exact worst-case concurrent Phase B scratch capacity, `W_B = 2,712` bytes (339 counter-block entries × 8; the three mapping tables ≤ 464 bytes each are covered; the counter block and the mapping tables never coexist, so the concurrent maximum is `max(2,712, 464) = 2,712`). **Implementation (V21 item 4): `W_B` is one physical `bytearray` of exactly 2,712 bytes allocated at ordinal 6 of the modeled allocation order; in Phase B the counter block and the one-at-a-time mapping tables are regions of this slab accessed by fixed numeric offsets with allocation-free reads/writes — no dict, map object, memoryview, array wrapper, or per-table buffer is created in Phase B; host interpreter temporaries (the bytearray object header, index/loop variables) are excluded under the fail-stop host boundary** | 2,712 once | **Phase A start (executed after the S8 hashing pass completes and all five one-at-a-time SHA-256 states are released — the first arena retention inside the six phases A–F after S8, never "the first retention of the pipeline phases" (V19-N2, resolved); at its reservation instant `used = 0`, proved by the explicit S8 release — immediately before the stage-1 parse pass) → end of Phase B; released immediately before the first Phase C retention.** Part of X (included in `used`, in `logical_arena_byte_count` at K1/K2, and in the `x_max` guard); not separate fixed overhead. Its reservation passes for every input (`used = 0` at that point; `0 + 2,712 = 2,712 <= 33,554,432`); its logical stage-46 candidate `0 + 2,712` is provably unable to breach `x_max`; **a physical `MemoryError` at the W_B allocation fires stage 48 at K1 with the pre-parse counters and `logical_arena_byte_count = 0` (pre-attempt `used`, named explicitly in every summary — V19-N1, V20 item 2, V21 item 2)** |
| **Live SHA-256 state (V19 item 1 extends the row; V20 item 2 assigns the Phase D stage-48 ownership; V21 item 2 scopes):** 256 logical bytes per live state; at most one live state at a time | 256 per live state | **S8 hashing pass (entry preflight): five inputs hashed one at a time in the fixed buffer order; each state retained through `reserve(256)` and released immediately after its buffer's hashing completes, before the next state is retained and before the W_B pre-reservation; after the fifth state is released `used = 0`. At every S8 retention `used = 0`, so the logical x_max guard cannot fire (`0 + 256 = 256 <= 33,554,432`, proved — the stage-46 firing point at K1 is provably unreachable); a physical `MemoryError` at an S8 state allocation fires stage 48 at K1 through the allocation-fault seam with `logical_arena_byte_count = 0` (pre-attempt `used`, named explicitly — V19-N1). Phase D stream hashing: the 27 stream digests are sequential, one state at a time, firing point at K4; a physical `MemoryError` at a Phase D state fires stage 48 at K4 with the complete counters and pre-attempt `used` = `parse-retained + full derivation charges` (V20 item 2, V21 item 2)** |
| Intern table: one mapping from distinct identifier byte-strings to intern ids | 16 bytes per mapping entry **plus** the exact UTF-8 byte length of each distinct identifier string (retained once) | whole call; **each input-identifier entry's allocation is a stage-46 firing point at K1 (candidate `used + n`) and a stage-48 firing point at K1 through the allocation-fault seam (position-appropriate counters, pre-attempt `used = W_B + parse_so_far`) — V20 item 2, scoped by V21 item 2**; ledger `obligation_gate_pairs`) and are streamed into their digest states without any derived-record retention — each such parsed record is charged exactly once under the parsed table and never again as a derived record. **Each derived-record allocation is a stage-46 firing point at K3 and a stage-48 firing point at K3 (V20 item 2, scoped by V21 item 2).** **Parsed-charge examples, scoped exactly (V17-N4 as applied by V18, resolved; Cardinality formulas: judge table exactly 46 entries (one per required obligation), aggregate table exactly 58 entries (one per gate), plumbing table exactly 58 entries (one per gate) — at stage 40 every owner has ≥ 1 pair (stages 31/35 and 39 passed) and the owners are exactly the in-domain entity sets (stage 29 passed). The order is frozen and traversal-independent: the parse-phase ordinals follow the frozen single-pass examination order (the same order in which the stage-1 parse pass admits records); the stage-2–6 sweep sort ordinals follow the frozen stage order; the derived case-id intern miss ordinals follow the frozen traversal order of V22 item 4 (between the last stage-2–6 sweep allocation and the first stage-7–28 evaluation sort allocation; a hit visit consumes no ordinal — V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5); the stage-7–28 evaluation ordinals follow the frozen stage order; the `h_max` physical-allocation ordinal is the single end-of-Phase-A ordinal; the Phase C ordinals follow the frozen derivation order; the Phase D ordinals follow the §2.4 table order; the output-builder preallocation ordinal is last. **Every modeled allocation point has exactly one checkpoint, one counter set, and one stage-48 value rule; Phase B has no modeled allocation point; the Phase D `h_max` use is a logical reservation with a stage-46 value rule at K4 and no stage-48 rule (V22 item 2); a case-id intern hit visit is not an allocation point and has no checkpoint, counter set, or stage-48 rule (V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5). Totality is scoped to the modeled inventory (V21 item 2): the compiler is not total over all physical allocations of the host — failure-result/diagnostic materialization and incidental host object/header allocations are outside the modeled inventory, and a native `MemoryError` at such an excluded allocation propagates unchanged as a fail-stop host failure outside the 48-variant algebra, never recursing into stage 48.**

**Allocation-fault seam (V19-N3, resolved by V20 item 5, scoped by V21 item 2, and extended by V22 items 2 and 4; V23 item 2 scopes the case-id intern ordinals to misses; V24 items 2–5 re-verify; V25 items 1–5 re-verify; V26 items 1–3 re-verify; frozen).** The tests-only allocation-fault helper (`tests/fixtures/compiler/fault_injection.py`) is armed with exactly one target ordinal `k` (a positive integer in the frozen 1-based modeled physical-allocation order above); it **fails exactly the k-th modeled bounded allocation once** — raising `MemoryError` after the logical reservation (the `reserve(n)` guard) succeeded — and then disarms. The seam: (i) is **unavailable in production** — it lives in a tests-only helper module, production modules are statically proven not to reference it, and production `compile()` never arms it; (ii) **does not alter traversal** — the allocation order, the guards, and the counters are identical whether or not the seam is armed, so every non-fault outcome is unchanged and identical inputs produce identical failure bytes for a given `k`; (iii) **targets exactly and only the enumerated modeled pipeline allocations** — there is no ordinal for the failure path (a stage-48-failed pipeline has already aborted, and failure-result/diagnostic materialization is outside the modeled inventory, so the seam cannot be armed there), **no ordinal exists for the Phase D `h_max` logical reservation (it is not a physical allocation — V22 item 2), and no ordinal exists for a case-id intern hit visit (it performs no modeled physical allocation — V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5)**; (iv) **exercises every K1/K3/K4 stage-48 branch** — ordinals 1–5 (S8), 6 (W_B), a parse ordinal (Phase A parse-time), a stage-2–6 sweep sort ordinal, a derived case-id intern miss ordinal (V22 item 4, V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5), a stage-7–28 evaluation ordinal, the single `h_max` ordinal, a Phase C derivation ordinal, a Phase D digest-state ordinal, and the output-builder preallocation ordinal each select their frozen class with the frozen counters of §6/§10; (v) has no ordinal inside Phase B (the sweep performs no modeled physical allocation — V20 item 2, V21 items 2 and 4); (vi) is the only mechanism by which stage 48 is exercised end-to-end (a bounded malformed input alone cannot deterministically raise `MemoryError` at a modeled allocation under the frozen caps — V19-N3, V20 item 5, V21 item 2). **The C4 collision fixture (V24 item 4) needs no seam: its hit visit is exercised by ordinary compilation, and its miss ordinals are the 424 case-id miss ordinals, any of which can be targeted by the seam for the stage-48 branch.**

**Frozen transient-structure high-water summary (exact, no `e.g.`):** during Phase B the only transient structures beyond the Phase A retained parse structures are the sweep counter block (2,712 bytes, a fixed-offset region of the W_B slab at Phase B start and live from Phase B start through stages 29–38, released before stage 39 — V19 item 6) and one stage-40 mapping table at a time (≤ 464 bytes, built after the counter block is released), all fixed-offset regions of the pre-reserved `W_B = 2,712`-byte slab (V17-B1 as applied by V18, V21 item 4). The Phase B transient high-water is therefore exactly 2,712 bytes (= W_B), and the Phase B peak concurrent charge is `parse-retained + W_B = parse-retained + 2,712` bytes — the value reported at checkpoint K2 (§6). `parse-retained` is the frozen charge of the Phase A structures that remain live (intern table — **including the derived case-id intern entries actually created by the miss visits of V22 item 4 (V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5); on the frozen collision-free basis all 425 visits are misses; a hit visit adds no entry** — plus parsed scalar/object/array slots plus pair-preserving duplicate facts), a pure function of the input bytes. **Because the W_B pre-reservation is part of X and is live from before the first parse retention, the Phase A guard admits parse retention only while `W_B + parse_so_far + n <= x_max`; therefore every Phase-B-admitted input satisfies `parse-retained <= x_max − W_B`, and `used = parse-retained + W_B <= x_max` at every Phase B instant — no stage-46 preemption can fire during Phase B (proven, not asserted); and because the sweep performs no modeled physical allocation, no stage-48 class exists in Phase B either (proven, not asserted — V20 item 2, V21 items 2 and 4).** The deterministic stage-46 first-exceed is exclusively a Phase A (K1), Phase C (K3), or Phase D/E/F (K4) property (including the Phase D `h_max` logical reservation at K4 — V22 item 2); the deterministic stage-48 first-failure is exclusively a Phase A (K1), Phase C (K3), or Phase D/E/F (K4) property over the modeled inventory, with every class's counter set frozen (§6, §10). **F46/B2″ termination (V24 item 1, preserved by V25 item 1): the Phase C reservation charges alone — `2,784 + 4,254·8,899 + 24·41,883 = 38,864,322 > 33,554,432` — exceed `x_max` even with zero parse-retained and zero case-id intern charge, so the deterministic stage-46 guard fires at or before the last Phase C reservation for every possible hit/miss sequence; the exact recurrence starts from the actual complete parse-retained charge over the exact fixture bytes and never from a displayed subtotal or lower bound.**

**Phase sequence (frozen; V14 item 9, V15-N3 as applied by V16, V17 item B2, V17-B1 as applied by V18, V19 items 1 and 6, V20 items 1–2, V21 items 1–4, V22 items 1–4, V23 items 1–2, V24 items 1–5, and V25 items 1–5).** (0) entry preflight S8 hashing pass: five SHA-256 states one at a time, each `reserve(256)` at `used = 0`, released before the next; one physical 2,712-byte `bytearray` at ordinal 6; stage-46 unreachable (`0 + 2,712`), stage-48 at K1 through the seam; (2) Phase A: stage-1 parse pass (parse-phase allocations are K1 46/48 modeled firing points), stages 2–6 sweep (sort-index allocations are K1 46/48 modeled firing points), **the late-Phase-A case-id intern substep (V22 item 4, V23 item 2, V24 items 2–5: 425 visits in the frozen traversal order, after all five documents complete structural and stage-6 admission, before stage 7; each visit performs an allocation-free lookup in the existing global intern table — a hit reuses the existing reference with 0 new arena bytes, no ordinal, and no firing point; a miss runs checked arithmetic, logically reserves exactly `16 + len(c)`, interns with that exact charge and is a K1 position-(f) 46/48 firing point; the general contract is 425 visits and zero to 425 allocations; the frozen collision-free basis/fixtures (V24 item 2; including the F46/B2″ synthetic expectation variant of V25 item 1) and the live basis (V24 item 3) have all 425 visits miss with the exact 26,618 charge; no CasePlan record)**, stages 7–28 evaluation (sort-index allocations are K1 46/48 modeled firing points with position (f) counters), then the source-commitment subphase (**the single physical `bytearray(65,536)` `h_max` allocation at subphase start — K1 46/48 modeled firing point with position (f) counters; four commitment preimages streamed; logical charge released after the subphase while the physical bytearray stays in a private reusable slot — V22 item 2**); (3) Phase B: validation-only sweep, stages 29–40, counter block carved from W_B at Phase B start and live through stages 29–38, released before stage 39; stage-40 mapping tables one at a time after the counter block is released; no modeled physical allocation, no ordinal, no stage-46/48 class; checkpoint K2; (4) Phase C: template-dependent derivation in the frozen order, each derived-record allocation a K3 46/48 modeled firing point, W_B released immediately before the first Phase C retention; checkpoint K3; (5) Phase D: the 27 digest streams one at a time through the shared `h_max` buffer, each SHA-256 state a K4 46/48 modeled firing point, **the Phase D `h_max` logical `reserve(65,536)` at K4 reusing the single Phase A physical bytearray (stage-46 firing point, no stage-48 class, no ordinal), the logical charge released after Phase D**; checkpoint K4; (6) Phase E: 14-stream k-way merge, the output-builder preallocation after the stage-47 candidate-total check (the last ordinal, K4, offset/view writes, never grows); (7) Phase F: final output serialization into the preallocated builder; stage 47 fires before the preallocation; checkpoint K4. **The F46/B2″ termination chain `2,784 + 4,254·8,899 + 24·41,883 = 38,864,322 > 33,554,432` (V24 item 1) is collision-independent and holds at Phase C for every possible hit/miss sequence; on the exact F46 fixture of V25 item 1, stages 1–40 pass in Phases A–B, Phase C is reached, and stage 46 fires at the first Phase C reservation whose candidate `used + n` exceeds `x_max`; on the exact B2″ fixture of V25 item 1, stage 40 fires at K2 in Phase B before Phase C.**

## 12. Causal/artifact partition and relation validation

### 12.1 Artifact causality and the exact partition (accepted parent, preserved)

The accepted parent freezes the exact raw/context/post-output artifact partition: **52 raw + 21 context + 22 post-output = 95**, with roles `manifest`/`environment` (context) and `report`/`checksums` (post-output). Every artifact membership is bound: `R_DE = 52` bundle/evidence membership records (per-lane `D = [3,1,4,4,5,4,5,5,6,7,4,4]`, `ΣD = 52`), `R_LC = 21` lane-context memberships (per-lane `LC = [1,2,2,1,2,2,2,2,2,1,2,2]`, `ΣLC = 21`), `R_LP = 22` post-gate-output memberships (per-lane `LP = [1,2,2,1,2,2,2,2,2,2,2,2]`, `ΣLP = 22`); `R_DE + R_LC + R_LP = 95`, preserving every required-artifact membership exactly once (accepted bundle v9 lines 431–432, re-verified). The expectation's `required_artifacts` lists (verified this revision from `expectations/turnvector-implementation-v2.json`, sha256 `e3127939…`): 9 lanes carry `environment` (scheduler-policy, scheduler-performance, mlx-native-correctness, bounded-turn-and-ffi, residency-and-memory-governor, cross-model-serving, observability-qualification, protocol-and-owner-lifecycle, certification-envelopes), 10 lanes carry `checksums` (scheduler-policy, scheduler-performance, mlx-native-correctness, bounded-turn-and-ffi, residency-and-memory-governor, cross-model-serving, observability-qualification, persistence-and-recovery, protocol-and-owner-lifecycle, certification-envelopes), artifact total 95. The compiler validates the partition totals and every per-lane membership against the ledger and the expectation `required_artifacts`; drift is `traceability_relation_mismatch` (stage 37, with the frozen exclusions). **The F46/B2″ synthetic expectation variant preserves the live `required_artifacts` sets verbatim, so the partition totals and the ledger memberships are unchanged on the family (V25 item 1, §15).**

Causal rank order (accepted parent, preserved): ranks 0–5 bind START(t) → CoveragePlan → receipt → TERMINAL(t) → finalized virtual-history tuple → RunEnvironment; ranks 6–14 bind lane-context, CaseResult, raw EvidenceMember, EvidenceBundle, JudgeResult, GateInputSet, GateResult, lane report, lane checksums; ranks 15–17 bind RunManifest/report/RunSeal. Every required predecessor edge has strictly increasing rank; a cycle would require `rank(x) < rank(x)`, impossible. CoveragePlan and receipt contain only the rank-0 through-START prefix; the rank-4 final-history tuple includes TERMINAL and is first persisted at rank 5.

### 12.2 Negative-test coverage functions (frozen; V12 item 2, V13 item 2, V15 items B2/N3, V16 items B1/N1, V17 items B1/N1)

- `R_HNJ = {(h, n_j(o))}` — total many-to-one; every path exactly one judge-negative template; the template id is the record `id` of the unique `judge_negative_tests` record whose `owner_id` is the path's obligation `o`. **Not a bijection** (46 templates, H ≥ 2,287 paths).
- `R_GNG = {(g, n_g(g))}` and `R_GNP = {(g, n_p(g))}` — the only gate-to-template bijections (58 gates ↔ 58 aggregate, 58 gates ↔ 58 plumbing); `n_g(g)`/`n_p(g)` are the record `id`s of the unique `aggregate_negative_tests`/`plumbing_negative_tests` records whose `owner_id` is the gate `g`. **On the F46/B2″ family the gate `g` is the 1,024-byte `fixture-gate-<NNNN>` form and the owner_id values are the same forms (V25 item 1).**
- Coverage checks (stage 40, Phase B, frozen subrule order): (1) template-class count drift (incl. empty array), then (2) mapping-count ≠ 1 (0 or ≥2), evaluated (2a) judge mappings in frozen path/owner order, then (2b) gate mappings in gate canonical order with per-gate aggregate-then-plumbing interleaving (V16-N1). The multiple-template condition and R_GNG/R_GNP non-bijection are consequences of subrule (2), never standalone primary predicates (V15-B2); template unboundness is owned by stages 31/35 (V15 item N3); a wrong owner is owned by stage 29.
- `compile-interruption/i` is custody quarantine state, never an expected claim key.

---

## 13. Output serialization and canonical emission order

Both outputs use compact recursively lexical-key JSON with `ensure_ascii=True` plus exactly one LF (§5, §6). Every object — top-level and nested — physically emits its existing keys in ascending lexical UTF-8 byte order; the field lists in §§5–6 document closed schemas and do not prescribe physical order. Arrays retain their declared canonical orders; object-key sorting never reorders an array. Integer values are canonical nonnegative decimal u64, digests lowercase 64-hex, and strings canonical escaped Unicode scalar strings. CoveragePlan and ContractFailure therefore each have one unambiguous canonical byte stream. The compiler writes through the `OutputSink` (q_max chunks into the final byte array; chunks are views, never separate retained buffers). **Output-builder freeze (V21 item 3): the output builder is one exact-size byte-array preallocation of the exact candidate total `P`, executed after the exact stage-47 candidate-total check passes and immediately before the first Phase E name write; it is the single final-output modeled allocation ordinal (the last ordinal, after the 27 Phase D digest states), uses checkpoint K4 with the complete counters, receives offset/view writes in Phases E and F, and never grows or reallocates; the allocation label and timing are identical in every statement of this design (§6 K4, §10 rows 47/48 and the phase sequence, §11 arena row and allocation-order row, §13, §15, §16, §21, §22).** The p-limit guard (`coverage_plan_or_failure_max` = 33,554,432) runs before publication on the exact candidate total and **before the output-builder preallocation**; stage 47 reports `output_byte_count_attempted` = the exact candidate total at the moment the guard trips in the frozen recursive lexical emission order (checkpoint K4), and a stage-47 failure produces no builder allocation (V21 item 3). The output-builder preallocation is a stage-48 firing point at K4 through the allocation-fault seam (complete counters, `output_byte_count_attempted = 0`; the builder is the P dimension, not charged to X) (V20 item 2, V21 items 2–3). **Fail-stop host boundary (V21 item 2): materializing a ContractFailure result (its value object, message bytes, diagnostic strings, and output bytes) and incidental host-language/runtime object or header allocations are outside the modeled bounded inventory — a native `MemoryError` raised while materializing any ContractFailure result, its diagnostic strings/output bytes, or excluded host bookkeeping propagates unchanged as a fail-stop host failure outside the 48-variant algebra, does not recurse into stage 48, and has no assigned checkpoint/counter.**

Python underscores and caller-frame checks are misuse-detection/convention boundaries under the accepted same-user trust model, never security boundaries and never claimed as unbypassable authority (V15 item N7).

## 15. Exhaustive boundary/negative tests

New test modules under `tests/` (unittest, repo pattern), plus `tests/fixtures/compiler/`:

- `tests/test_compiler_fixture_utils.py` — fixture generator: builds a synthetic canonical catalog with exactly 46 required obligations and no optional/out-of-scope record, joining the **real** expectation v2 with the real `(lane, behavior_case_id)` pairs and per-lane counts; it must not copy the PR 5 fixture's optional `synthetic-case-0047` pattern. It uses synthetic source files under `tests/fixtures/compiler/source/` with recomputed digests, a synthetic canonical `traceability-v1.json` (real gate ids/case ids, synthetic template ids, `binds` with the real expectation digest and synthetic others), a synthetic `AuthoritySnapshot` (including a valid `RepositoryControlDescriptor` with `schema_version` const `turnvector.benchmark.repository-control.v1` and `strict_parser_version` const `canonical-strict-parser-v1`), and a synthetic `CompilePermit` issued through `test_permit_issuer.py`. The real expectation file digest `e3127939…` is bound in the ledger fixture. **Frozen synthetic-identifier construction (V24 item 2, preserved by V25 item 1):** every synthetic identifier other than the deliberate C4 collision begins exactly `fixture-<domain>-` followed by a zero-padded four-digit ordinal; the domain-tag table is frozen:
  | Domain | Tag | Count | Form |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | long obligations (F46/B2″ family) | `fixture-obligation-` | 46 | `<NNNN>` + 1001 `x` → exactly 1,024 bytes |
  | long judges (F46/B2″ family) | `fixture-judge-` | 46 | `<NNNN>` + 1006 `x` → exactly 1,024 bytes |
  | long bundles (F46/B2″ family) | `fixture-bundle-` | 12 | `<NNNN>` + 1005 `x` → exactly 1,024 bytes |
  | long gates (F46/B2″ family) | `fixture-gate-` | 58 | `<NNNN>` + 1007 `x` → exactly 1,024 bytes |
  | judge templates | `fixture-template-judge-` | 46 | `<NNNN>` (0001..0046) |
  | aggregate templates | `fixture-template-aggregate-` | 58 | `<NNNN>` (0001..0058) |
  | plumbing templates | `fixture-template-plumbing-` | 58 | `<NNNN>` (0001..0058) |
  | evidence sources | `fixture-source-` | 41 | `<NNNN>` (0001..0041) |
  | base obligations | `fixture-obligation-` | 46 | `<NNNN>` (unpadded) |
  | base judges | `fixture-judge-` | 46 | `<NNNN>` (unpadded) |
  | base bundles | `fixture-bundle-` | 12 | `<NNNN>` (unpadded) |
  | base gates | `fixture-gate-` | 58 | `<NNNN>` (unpadded) |
  | catalog header | `fixture-catalog-v1` | 1 | frozen literal |
  | ledger | `fixture-ledger-v1` | 1 | frozen literal |
  | catalog seam ids | `fixture-seam-` | per fixture | `<NNNN>` |
  | catalog claim classes | `fixture-claim-` | per fixture | `<NNNN>` |
  | reconciliation synthetic ids (only if a synthetic reconciliation is emitted) | `fixture-reconciliation-` | per fixture | `<NNNN>` |
  | snapshot synthetic identifiers (only if any are introduced) | `fixture-snapshot-` | per fixture | `<NNNN>` |

  Every generated case id begins with one of the 12 real lane ids followed by `.`, so no `fixture-`-prefixed identifier can equal one (prefix-disjoint; the 162 long ones are also length-disjoint at 1,024 bytes vs the 34–55-byte case ids). Frozen consts (e.g. `catalog`, `turnvector-implementation-v2`, `turnvector.benchmark.traceability.v1`, `ascii-casefold-v1`) and real bound values (the expectation ids, the reconciliation ids, 40-hex revisions, 64-hex digests) are admitted as-is and covered by the completeness assertion. **Complete prior-intern-set assertion (V24 item 2, frozen as part of fixture generation, not an optional check; wording corrected by V25 item 2):** the generator computes the complete prior-intern set under the exact enumeration rule of V25 item 2 — every JSON string value in all five exact fixture inputs matching the stable identifier grammar `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]*$`, excluding the lowercase 64-hex class (`^[0-9a-f]{64}$`) — and asserts its intersection with the 425 generated case-id set is empty before the fixture bytes are emitted/compiled; a non-empty intersection is a hard fixture-generation failure. **The 64-hex exclusion is justified by the structural disjointness proof of V25 item 2, not by field typing: every generated case id is 34–55 bytes and contains literal dots (two), while every lowercase 64-hex string is exactly 64 bytes and contains no dot, so no 64-hex value can equal a generated case id and the exclusion cannot hide a genuine collision; the enumerated set is a superset of the identifier values actually interned from the fixtures for the case-id collision check, with the 64-hex class excluded by structural disjointness. It is not claimed to be a literal superset of every schema-valid identifier value, and a 64-hex string is not claimed to be necessarily digest-typed.** For the C4 collision fixture the frozen assertion takes the exact expected-intersection form: `prior_intern ∩ case_ids == {core-event-replay.effect-result-replay.0001}` (cardinality exactly one, equal to the first generated case id). **The fixture's three negative-template arrays are exactly two-field `{owner_id, id}` records: `judge_negative_tests` uses obligation ids as `owner_id` (46 records), `aggregate_negative_tests` and `plumbing_negative_tests` use gate ids as `owner_id` (58 records each), and `id` values are the synthetic template ids (`fixture-template-*`); no fixture record carries `template_id` or `gate_id`; every template `id` is distinct within its array (V12 item 2, V13 item 2).** The generator also emits the stage-46 fixture family of §10 (F46 and B2″): the 162 long obligation/judge/bundle/gate identifiers of exactly 1,024 bytes (the frozen `fixture-`-tagged padded forms above), complete-bipartite pairs, and (for B2″) the compensated judge drift; **the F46/B2″ family binds the synthetic expectation variant of V25 item 1 — NOT the unchanged live expectation bytes, NOT the live digest `e3127939…`, and NOT the real gate ids — so the case ids are the actual 425 generated ids with their exact lengths (identical to the live basis because the variant preserves the lane/matrix/dimension structure; V22 item 1, V25 item 1); the generator emits the golden ordered case-id list from `lane_contract.py`'s traversal (expectation lane order → matrix order → Cartesian-product dimension/value order, ordinals continuing across matrices within a lane)**. **The C4 collision fixture (V24 item 4) is a separate otherwise-valid fixture whose synthetic ledger judge id is exactly `core-event-replay.effect-result-replay.0001` (the first generated case id in frozen order, 43 bytes), with every input-side reference to that judge consistent and unique within the judge domain (the other 45 judge ids are `fixture-judge-0001`…`fixture-judge-0045`) and every other synthetic identifier `fixture-`-prefixed and disjoint; its exact expected prior-intern intersection is the single first case id.**

  **Frozen F46/B2″ synthetic expectation variant (V25 item 1, normative; closes V24-B1).** The generator emits a synthetic expectation variant derived from the live `expectations/turnvector-implementation-v2.json` bytes with exactly this construction:
  - **Preserved byte-for-byte where applicable:** top-level `schema_version` (`turnvector.benchmark.expectation.v3`), `id` (`turnvector-implementation-v2`), `description`, `source_contract` (`repository` `TurnVector`, `revision` `eedad5faf881da329844463eeaf54d9970350abd`, `clean_required` true), `authority` (`source_reconciliation_path` `authority/source-reconciliation-v1.json`, `source_reconciliation_sha256` `13706cdd46416d394fe3b8c9ce47203c7b77367199be31f502eb3b7122db7a1c`), `certification_policy`; each of the 12 lanes in declaration order with `id`/`layer`/`description`/`required`/`implementation_sources`/`harness`/`matrices`/`cases`/`required_artifacts`/`claim_scope`; every matrix `id` and `dimensions`; every dimension `id`/`values` (119 string + 29 numeric values, unchanged); every behavior-case record; the lanes' declaration orders of every array.
  - **Replaced:** each lane's `gates` array's gate-record `id` field values only (the gate records' `metric`/`operator`/`expected`/`unit`/`threshold_source`/`evidence` fields unchanged), replaced by the 1,024-byte `fixture-gate-<NNNN>` form.
  - **Ordinal convention (single global):** iterate lanes in declaration order (indices 0..11); within a lane, iterate the lane's gate records in their declaration order; assign the next unused ordinal 0001..0058. The frozen per-lane slices are: lane 0 `core-event-replay` (3 gates): 0001–0003; lane 1 `scheduler-policy` (3): 0004–0006; lane 2 `scheduler-performance` (4): 0007–0010; lane 3 `request-serving-lifecycle` (5): 0011–0015; lane 4 `mlx-native-correctness` (4): 0016–0019; lane 5 `bounded-turn-and-ffi` (4): 0020–0023; lane 6 `residency-and-memory-governor` (7): 0024–0030; lane 7 `cross-model-serving` (7): 0031–0037; lane 8 `observability-qualification` (7): 0038–0044; lane 9 `persistence-and-recovery` (4): 0045–0048; lane 10 `protocol-and-owner-lifecycle` (5): 0049–0053; lane 11 `certification-envelopes` (5): 0054–0058. Per-lane counts `[3,3,4,5,4,4,7,7,7,4,5,5]` sum to 58; the 58 `fixture-gate-<NNNN>` ids are pairwise distinct.
  - **Form:** `fixture-gate-` (13 bytes) + the 4-digit zero-padded ordinal + 1,007 lowercase `x` bytes = exactly 1,024 bytes; grammar-valid `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]*$`; within a lane the byte-lexical order of the gate ids equals the ordinal order (identical prefix), so the ledger `gates` array sorted by `(lane_id, gate_id)` and the stage-37/40 gate canonical order are deterministic.
  - **Canonicalization:** the variant is emitted with the same grandfathered non-lexical object-key layout as the live file (stage-6 rule (vi) exempts the expectation's object-key layout from re-encoding); strict UTF-8, LF, final LF, no duplicate keys, no floats/surrogates, strict types; arrays in their schema-ordered declaration orders (lanes, matrices, dimensions, dimension values, cases, gates — each in its live declaration order, unchanged by the replacement). The variant's exact bytes are the fixture's expectation input; `D_exp_syn` = SHA-256 over them, computed by the generator.
  - **Resulting case ids:** because the case ids depend only on lane id, matrix id, dimension values, and the generator ordinal (all unchanged), the 425 case ids, their traversal order, per-lane counts `[20,3,40,15,18,36,80,36,36,22,24,95]`, length distribution `{34:12, 35:6, 37:36, 41:67, 42:12, 43:20, 45:16, 47:15, 48:119, 50:6, 53:24, 54:12, 55:80}`, min 34, max 55, and exact intern charge 26,618 (on the collision-free fixture) are identical to the live basis (recomputed this revision, §16).
  - **Digest ownership and binding fields (frozen):** `D_exp_syn` is bound in the ledger `binds.expectation_sha256`, the catalog header `expectation_sha256`, the permit payload `expectation_sha256`, and (indirectly) `input_set_sha256`; the binding fields whose canonical preimages change relative to the base fixture are exactly those whose preimage includes the expectation bytes (the expectation input bytes themselves; the derived digest streams that project gate ids — `gate`, `obligation_gate`, `path_gate`, `enforcement_path`, `gate_aggregate_negative`, `gate_plumbing_negative` — change their value bytes, but the F46/B2″ fixtures never reach Phase D/E, so no plan bytes are produced). The live expectation digest `e3127939…` and the real gate ids are **intentionally not reused** by the F46/B2″ family; no statement in this design claims the family binds the unchanged live expectation bytes, the unchanged live digest, or the unchanged real gate ids.
  - **Source-reconciliation input of the F46/B2″ family:** the live `authority/source-reconciliation-v1.json` bytes (sha256 `13706cdd46416d394fe3b8c9ce47203c7b77367199be31f502eb3b7122db7a1c`), bound as-is. It points to the synthetic expectation through its unchanged `successor_expectation.id` (`turnvector-implementation-v2`) and `successor_expectation.schema_version` (`turnvector.benchmark.expectation.v3`) — equal to the variant's retained top-level identity fields; its `target_source` (`repository` `TurnVector`, `clean_required` true, `revision` `eedad5f…`) equals the variant's `source_contract` (unchanged); its seven mappings and `predecessor_expectation` are unchanged (they reference no gate id and no v2-expectation bytes); the variant's `authority.source_reconciliation_sha256` = `13706cdd…` equals this input's digest (stage 16 passes). No reconciliation field changes; the reconciliation's 59-occurrence/25-distinct non-hex enumeration is unchanged (V25 item 3) and its exact occurrence decomposition `7 + 17 + 7 + 7 + 16 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 = 59` is re-verified by V26 item 1.
  - **Synthetic catalog of the family:** header `expectation_sha256` = `D_exp_syn`, `source_reconciliation_sha256` = `13706cdd…`, `design_gate_revision`/`compile_custody_policy_sha256`/`custody_domain_sha256` = the fixture's frozen values (same as the base fixture), `predecessor` = null, `record_count` = 47; 46 required obligation records joining the real `(lane_id, behavior_case_id)` pairs; `source_file_sha256`/`section_sha256` recomputed over the unchanged synthetic source files.
  - **Synthetic ledger of the family:** `binds.expectation_sha256` = `D_exp_syn`; `binds.source_reconciliation_sha256` = `13706cdd…`; `binds.catalog_sha256` = `D_cat`; `gates` entity = the 58 `fixture-gate-<NNNN>` ids (per-lane slices above); `obligation_gate_pairs` = the complete-bipartite assignment `m_l = O_l·G_l` (`m_l = [12,9,12,20,16,16,28,28,28,16,20,20]`, `M = 225`, `H = 8,899`, `R_HE = 41,883`) referencing the fixture-gate ids; `judge_negative_tests` (46, `owner_id` = obligation ids), `aggregate_negative_tests` (58, `owner_id` = fixture-gate ids), `plumbing_negative_tests` (58, `owner_id` = fixture-gate ids); `binds.gate_sha256` keyed by the 58 fixture-gate ids; `binds.judge_negative_sha256`/`aggregate_negative_sha256`/`plumbing_negative_sha256` keyed by the fixture-template ids; `cardinalities` with the frozen counts (gate_count 58, lane_gate_counts `[3,3,4,5,4,4,7,7,7,4,5,5]`, obligation_gate_pair_count 225, enforcement_path_count 8,899, path_evidence_record_count 41,883); `design_gate_revision` = the fixture value; `predecessor` = null.
  - **Synthetic snapshot of the family:** `repository` = `TurnVector`, `clean_required` = true, `revision` = `eedad5f…` (unchanged); `source_files`/`section_records`/`historical_objects` matching the synthetic catalog (unchanged content/digests); `repository_control` = the valid descriptor (unchanged).
  - **Live identities intentionally not reused:** the live expectation file bytes and digest (`e3127939…`); the real gate ids (`replay-hash`, `invariant-safety`, …, `failed-transition-effects`). **Reused (do not need to change):** the live reconciliation bytes and digest (`13706cdd…`); the real lane/behavior-case/matrix/dimension ids and values; the real required-artifact sets; the real revision values; the frozen consts. Every prior statement that the F46/B2″ family binds "the unchanged live expectation", "the unchanged live digest", or "the real gate ids" is removed.
  - **Lane-by-lane gate-set construction (stage 37 equality is immediate):** for every lane, the synthetic expectation variant's per-lane gate set EQUALS the synthetic ledger's per-lane `gates` set — both are the same `fixture-gate-<NNNN>` ordinal slice of §15 — so stage 37's per-lane gate-set equality is satisfied by construction (identical declared sets), with per-lane counts `[3,3,4,5,4,4,7,7,7,4,5,5]`.
  - **Templates, gate bindings, path gate foreign keys, ordering keys, pointers, duplicate rules, and counts affected by the replacement (all frozen):** the aggregate/plumbing negative templates' `owner_id` = the fixture-gate ids (58 each); `binds.gate_sha256` keys = the fixture-gate ids; the enforcement-path `gate_id` field and the `path_gate` stream's `gate_id` = the fixture-gate id; the h composite's `g` field = the 1,024-byte fixture-gate id (the 1,024-byte contribution to `len(h) = 4·1,024 + len(c) + 4`); the `gates` entity array sorted by `(lane_id, gate_id)` (byte-lexical = ordinal within a lane); `obligation_gate_pairs` sorted by `(lane_id, obligation_id, gate_id)`; the stage-35 pointer `/traceability_ledger/entities/gates/<index>/gate_id` unchanged (field name unchanged); stage-5 gate-id distinctness (58 distinct); stage-30 duplicate complete gate entity record; stage-40 subrule (2b) gate mappings in gate canonical `(lane_id, gate_id)` order with per-gate aggregate-then-plumbing interleaving; counts: gate_count 58, lane_gate_counts `[3,3,4,5,4,4,7,7,7,4,5,5]`, M = 225, H = 8,899, R_HE = 41,883.
  - **End-to-end reachability tests (V25 item 1):** the F46 test asserts stages 1–40 pass on the exact F46 fixture (the full stage walk of §10 F46), that Phase C is reached, and that stage 46 fires at the first Phase C reservation whose candidate `used + n` exceeds `x_max` per the deterministic recurrence, with the collision-independent chain asserted as a lower bound with no displayed subtotal; the B2″ test asserts stages 1–39 pass, stage 37 passes, and stage 40 subrule (2a) fires at checkpoint K2 before any later resource check (the potential Phase C total is never reached); both tests assert the synthetic-variant digest bindings (`binds.expectation_sha256` = `D_exp_syn` etc.) and the lane-by-lane gate-set equality.

- `tests/test_traceability_ledger.py` — schema: unknown field, duplicate key, float, NaN/Infinity, surrogate, boolean-as-integer (stage 2), u64 out of range (stage 4), invalid identifier, unsorted/duplicate records, wrong counts, wrong per-lane arrays, wrong bind key sets, predecessor mismatch shape (including a predecessor whose `compile_custody_lineage_id` equals the current lineage → stage 23), declared-array checked-sum overflow (stage 41 over admitted entries); **negative-template arrays with multiple templates per owner remain ordered by `(owner_id, id)`; a negative-template record carrying a third field (`template_id` or `gate_id`) is rejected at stage 2 as an unknown field; a repeated template `id` — under a different `owner_id` or under the same `owner_id` as an exact duplicate `{owner_id,id}` record — fires stage 5 as the sole owner of repeated negative-template ids; an unsorted `(owner_id,id)` array whose `id` values are all distinct fires stage 6 (stage 6 skips any array containing a repeated id); a duplicate complete ledger gate entity record fires stage 30, and the three negative-template arrays are explicitly excluded from stage 30 (V12 item 2, V13 item 2)**; **a stage-40 count-drift fixture (45 judge templates) fires subrule (1); a compensated mapping-drift fixture (one obligation with two distinct judge templates, another with none — total 46) fires subrule (2a) at the first path of the first offending obligation in the frozen path/owner order; a compensated gate fixture (one gate with two aggregate templates, another with none — total 58) fires subrule (2b) at the first offending gate with per-gate aggregate-then-plumbing interleaving; a fixture with an aggregate defect at a late gate and a plumbing defect at an early gate asserts the per-gate interleaved first-offender selection (V16-N1, V17 items B1/N1); the multiple-template condition never fires as a standalone predicate; **a stage-40 failure asserts that no R_HNJ/R_GNG/R_GNP record exists anywhere before stage 40 passes (V17 item B2)**; **the B2″ fixture of V25 item 1 (the compensated judge drift on the synthetic expectation variant) asserts stage 40 subrule (2a) fires at the first path of the first offending obligation with stage 37 having passed**.
- `tests/test_authority_snapshot.py` — every path/type/marker/revision/clean/digest/range/historical/descriptor rule and one-field drift; `ControlFileIdentity` shape (`size==byte_count`, u64 fields, type regular); **a type-correct `SourceFileRecord.no_follow_identity.size != byte_count` with admitted u64 values → stage 19 at `/authority_snapshot/source_files/<i>/no_follow_identity/size` (V14 item 1)**; `StabilityIdentity` shape (size-bearing, 8 u64 fields); root-record 11-key shape and identity 9-key shape; **root records as logical `(namespace, "")` parents** (witness parent lookup for `Q=""`, scope-root lookups, parent-chain termination, entry counts); namespace ownership when roots are identical; **both worktree kinds**: a `worktree_kind=linked` descriptor modeled on the observed checkout (commondir/gitdir present with bound bytes/identities) and a `worktree_kind=main` descriptor (commondir/gitdir explicit absences with component-probe witnesses); **no `common_dir/worktrees` logical records and no fabricated absent stability entries**; component-probe witness golden recomputation (`parent_stability_record_sha256` recomputation over the root or present-directory parent, exact path reconstruction, missing/extra/duplicate witness rejection, key/locator mismatch rejection, non-directory intermediate and symlink fail-closed cases); **the exact witness/scope reference graph and consumer sets** (each witness exactly one consumer; each scope its exact consumer set; missing/extra/duplicate edges rejected; the three two-consumer scope records `common-refs`, `common-replace-refs`, `worktree-control` asserted with both consumers); **the frozen `common-replace-refs` state machine (V9 disposition item 1): the admitted absent-root shape asserted with `witness_ref`, `entry_count=0`, and the empty-stream digest `e3b0c442…` and with `W(common_dir,"refs/replace")` having exactly one consumer (the absent-root scope record); the admitted present-empty present-root shape asserted with `entry_count=1` and the single root stability record; every non-admitted shape rejected (V9 disposition item 1)**. **V28 descriptor/config/command goldens:** identity objects assert exact nine-key shape (one absolute string plus eight u64), root records equal the corresponding identity object member-for-member, and accepted `/` plus rejected relative, trailing-slash, empty-interior, `.`, `..`, NUL, and noncanonical-UTF-8 absolute paths point at Stage 19 while Stage 7 remains non-descriptor-only. Config tests assert the literal `0\n`, `false\n`, and `true\n` preimages and all three frozen hashes; boolean aliases normalize to the same JSON booleans; invalid tokens/escapes, includes, continuations, duplicates, and unknown grammar are rejected; opaque strings hash canonical JSON string bytes without inversion; and absent/false/true `extensions.worktreeConfig` cases assert exact `config.worktree` control, witness, Class-C `Hcw`, namespace-entry, and lock-proof coupling. Command tests preserve the exact command-id vocabulary/order/8-key shape/returncode/stderr/count/digest/projection checks and prove `argv` is only a canonical strict-string-array provenance binding: two different structurally valid arrays pass when all frozen non-argv predicates agree, while mistyped/non-string/noncanonical arrays fail; no predetermined-command-content or substitution equality oracle exists.
- `tests/test_compile_permit.py` — single consume, second consume raises `permit_reuse`, copy/deepcopy/pickle prohibited, process-boundary mismatch (PID fail-stop), input-set digest mismatch (including a wrong byte-count field), byte-count identity mismatch, non-frozen limits, attempt bounds, production-token frame guard, test-token frame guard, test permit rejected by `compile()` (**the stored-token identity check S4/S5, V14 item 6: a test permit carrying `_TEST_ISSUER_TOKEN` raises `compiler_identity_mismatch` at `compile()`; a production permit raises `CompilerInternalError` at `_compile_test`; issuance with the wrong token raises the sole `CompilerInternalError`**), **PermitPayload construction boundary (V14 item 7): a payload with an out-of-u64-domain byte count (negative, ≥ 2^64, or non-integer) cannot be constructed and raises `CompilerPreconditionViolation("input_identity_mismatch")` before any compiler call; the consumption-boundary byte-count comparison uses the actual O(1) `nbytes` (§4 step S7)**; **token disclosure wording (V15 item N7): the tests assert the frame guards as misuse-detection, and an import-level test documents that a same-user module can read the module-level token attributes directly — no unbypassable authority is claimed**; **the F46/B2″ permit bindings (V25 item 1): the permit payload's `expectation_sha256` = `D_exp_syn` and `input_set_sha256` recomputed over the synthetic inputs, asserted consistent with the catalog header and ledger binds**. **V28 exception assertions:** issuance-token mismatch, PID mismatch, S5, caller-frame/host invariant, and private encoder/decoder invariant failures all raise exactly `CompilerInternalError`; the test asserts that it is not a `CompilerPreconditionViolation`, is never serialized, has no alias/subclass alternative, and is disjoint from every ContractFailure variant. `issuance_kind` is asserted absent from both output schemas and bytes and retained only as internal bookkeeping/fixture provenance; a same-user direct token import and valid-token factory call is explicitly possible and not treated as a security-boundary breach.

The B2″ fixture of V25 item 1 additionally asserts stage 37 passes on the synthetic expectation variant before stage 40 fires.**

- `tests/test_compiler_resources.py` — reachable end-to-end exact/one-past guards plus pure accounting-helper tests for genuinely shadowed maxima. **The catalog 512/513 boundary is end-to-end reachable at stage 24** (a 513-record synthetic catalog fires `catalog_record_limit_exceeded` **after full admission** before later semantic cardinality checks; 512 records pass stage 24), so the claim that this maximum is shadowed is removed. **The 1,024/1,025 section boundary is end-to-end reachable at stage 44 during admission (V15-N6 as applied by V16): a 1,025th structurally valid section-bearing obligation record preempts at stage 44 with the through-offender prefix, before full admission and before stage 24; an implementer that evaluated stage 24 incrementally during admission would make the stage-44 branch unreachable — the timing is frozen as stage 24 = post-full-admission, stage 44 = admission-time.** Only genuinely shadowed path/entity maxima — `path_count_max` 32,768 (forced below by the pair bounds), `case_plan_count_max` 4,096 (forced below by the frozen per-lane case counts), the derived-record degree caps (d_H/d_CH/d_GH under the frozen profile), and the obligation/judge/bundle/source/gate count maxima under the frozen 46/58/41/12 profile — remain pure accounting-helper tests. The explicit `memoryview(bytearray(...)).toreadonly()` test is read-only yet rejected before payload access because the ultimate exporter is mutable; a matched bytes-backed view reaches the Stage-45 multidimensional boundary with zero pre-cap traversal.

The existing verification contract commands are unchanged for every PR.
